### Story 87.3: The host forwards Herald's webhooks to the mcp-host sidecar

As the operator running the platform host from the image env (`python-agent-platform`, `platform-dev`), which cannot hold `pyforge-herald` beside Langflow,
I want herald's webhook served by the mcp-host sidecar and reached through the host,
So that a signed ship reaches herald with no change to the image env, and herald Story 19.2's proof runs host plus sidecar on loopback.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** S-87.1 (its absent-herald 404 is this story's fallback) • **FR/AD:** `spec-pyforge-steward` CAP-68 (isolated mcp-host env) and CAP-69 (host proxies; sidecar down is loud), both from `spec-mcp-era-isolation`; CAP-10 of `spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy` kept through 87.1; pap:AD-2 • Dream 2026-10-10 (herald via the sidecar) • gates herald Story 19.2's live proof (cross-station; herald holds the gate as its own `blocked` row) • deferred row `DW-steward-87-3-1`
**Flag:** `pyforge.steward.herald_webhook_sidecar` (openfeature-file, global; production off, staging off, dev on; fallback: Story 87.1's behaviour, nothing forwarded; cleanup 90 days after ON everywhere)
**Surface:** `pixi.toml` (`[feature.mcp-host.dependencies]` gains the `pyforge-herald` path dependency; `[feature.mcp-host.tasks]` gains `mcp-host-serve`), `pixi.lock` (the `mcp-host` block only), `environment.yaml`, `src/platform/mcp_host/app.py`, `src/platform/config/asgi.py`, `src/platform/config/flags.json` and `flag-overlays.json`, `src/shared/packages/django-pyforge/src/django_pyforge/mcp_http.py` or a new `django_pyforge/sidecar_forward.py`, `src/platform/tests/` (new tests; `test_openfeature_file_flags.py`), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-core/tests/unit/test_flags.py`, `docs/explanation/platform-deployment-architecture.md`, the derived `docs/reference/environments.md`, `docs/foundry/sbom-gaps.md`, `docs/reference/library-llms-full.md` and `docs/governance/flag-inventory/` (through their tools), steward's `deferred-work-ledger.md` (one follow-up row).
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-87-3-the-host-forwards-herald-s-webhooks-to-the-mcp-host-sidecar.md`
**Given** `99bc8f3cab`, where `pyforge-herald` needs `mcp >=2.2.0` and `langflow-base` / `lfx` 1.12.3 pin `mcp <2.0.0` (the image env and `platform-dev` lock `mcp 1.28.1`), Story 87.1 answers herald's paths with a 404 that says herald is not installed, and the mcp-host sidecar (`mcp 2.2.0`) already receives the host's `POST /stations/<name>/mcp` when `MCP_HOST_SIDECAR_BASE_URL` is set
**When** `[feature.mcp-host.dependencies]` gains `pyforge-herald`, `mcp_host/app.py` serves `/stations/herald/api/v1/webhooks/*` with herald's `webhook_host.application` (loaded by module name), and the host, with the sidecar URL set and the flag ON, forwards that path family to the sidecar
**Then** a signed `on-ship` sent to the host reaches the sidecar with its method, path, query and body byte-identical and `content-type`, `X-Hub-Signature-256` and `X-Hub-Timestamp` intact, never `Authorization` or `Cookie`; herald verifies the signature in the sidecar; the caller gets the sidecar's status and body; an unreachable sidecar is a 502 and one ERROR log. With the flag OFF or the URL unset, the host behaves exactly as today, in-process herald or 87.1's 404, and herald's non-webhook routes are never forwarded. In the sidecar, an absent herald is a 404 naming the module and a missing `HERALD_REPO_ROOT` or `HERALD_WEBHOOK_SECRET` is a 503 naming the variable, with `/health` and the MCP faces untouched.
**And** the lock moves through `pixi lock` only, and only the `mcp-host` environment block changes (`mcp` 2.x, `psycopg` 3.2.10, no `postgresql` or `libpq` at 18). `pixi run --frozen -e mcp-host mcp-host-serve` binds `127.0.0.1:8090` and answers `/health`. MCP forwarding is unchanged (`test_mcp_host_sidecar.py`, `test_mcp_transport_auth.py` unedited). The flag is registered in its four places, and `flag-gate-check` passes. The docs name the route, the flag, the allowlist and the local task. The chart's `platform.mcpHostEnv`, compose's `mcp-host` service, the store's volume and the paused image build become one steward deferred-work row. `environment.yaml` and the derived docs come from their tools. `pyforge-steward-test`, `pyforge-station-tests`, `platform-ci-local -- --test`, `lint-types`, `sbom-gaps-check` and `llms-full-check` are green, and every Spec `spec-surface-check` names gets a memlog entry and one scoped stamp.
**Status:** done

### Story 87.4: The herald webhook forward closes its review findings

As the operator who landed Story 87.3 with its independent review's findings unfixed and turned its forward dark,
I want the host to forward only herald's two webhook paths, read no more than herald would, and prove it through its real entry point,
So that the forward can come back ON in dev without opening the sidecar's MCP faces, and herald 19.2's proof can run.

**Type:** fix • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** S-87.3 • **FR/AD:** `spec-pyforge-steward` CAP-68 and CAP-69 (both from `spec-mcp-era-isolation`), the CAPs Story 87.3 bound; pap:AD-2 • Dream 2026-10-10 (herald forward review findings) • operator ruling 2026-10-10 "Forward-fix (Recommended)" • gates herald Story 19.2's live proof in place of 87.3 (cross-station; herald holds the gate as its own `blocked` row)
**Flag:** none of its own (a fix, spec-feature-flag-governance Q1); it turns 87.3's `pyforge.steward.herald_webhook_sidecar` back ON in dev, last, and leaves staging and production OFF
**Surface:** `src/shared/packages/django-pyforge/src/django_pyforge/sidecar_forward.py` (`is_herald_webhook_path` `:38`-`:39`, `_sidecar_url` `:51`-`:57`, `proxy_herald_webhook` `:79`-`:118`), `src/platform/mcp_host/app.py` (`_dispatch_herald_webhook` `:43`-`:63`), `src/platform/config/asgi.py` (only if the forward branch at `:135` must move), `pixi.toml` (`[feature.mcp-host.tasks]` `mcp-host-serve`, `:401`), `environment.yaml` (through its export only), `src/platform/config/flag-overlays.json`, `src/platform/tests/` (`test_herald_webhook_sidecar.py`, `test_openfeature_file_flags.py`, new tests), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-core/tests/unit/test_flags.py`, 87.3's spec (`flag.default` and its **Flag** bullet only), `docs/explanation/platform-deployment-architecture.md`, `docs/governance/flag-inventory/` (through its tool)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-87-4-the-herald-webhook-forward-closes-its-review-findings.md`
**Given** `c183207e67`, where 87.3 is `done` on `main` (merge `85e70dedf5`) and its spec's Review Triage Log records "2026-10-10 — Independent review, iteration 1: FAIL (3 high, 3 medium)", and `pyforge.steward.herald_webhook_sidecar` is OFF in every environment since the 2026-10-10 hygiene change. The forwarder matches only the prefix `/stations/herald/api/v1/webhooks/` and appends the client's decoded path and query to the sidecar URL, so `..`, `%2e%2e`, `%3F`, `%23` and `%0d%0a` reach the sidecar's unauthenticated MCP faces or forge a log line. It reads the body with no cap. `mcp-host-serve` exits 1 (`ModuleNotFoundError: django_pyforge`). No test drives `config.asgi.application`, and the sidecar reports any missing module as herald absent
**When** the host forwards with the flag ON and `MCP_HOST_SIDECAR_BASE_URL` set
**Then** only herald's `ON_SHIP_PATH` and `ON_PR_CLOSE_PATH` are forwarded, to the matched literal with no query string, and any other path under the prefix gets the host's 404 with nothing sent and no log record carrying the path. A body over herald's caps (1,000,000 bytes, 10,000 messages) gets a 413 before the sidecar is contacted. A test loads herald by module name and asserts the literals and caps equal herald's constants. `mcp-host-serve` binds `127.0.0.1:8090` and answers `/health` 200. The sidecar answers 404 only for a missing `pyforge.herald` module and re-raises any other
**And** tests through `httpx.ASGITransport` on `config.asgi.application` prove 87.3's ACs 4, 6 and 7, and AC 3's herald-absent case uses a meta-path finder. Only after those pass, in the same commit set, the overlay, both flag-default tables and 87.3's flag block read dev ON again (staging and production OFF), `test_herald_webhook_sidecar_flag_two_states` stays green, and `flag-gate-check` passes. MCP forwarding, `test_host_boots_without_herald.py` and the image env are unchanged, `pixi.lock` does not move, and `environment.yaml` equals its stdout-only export. Matching the prefix, building the URL from the client, dropping a cap, deleting the `asgi.py` branch or catching every `ModuleNotFoundError` each fails a new test (mutation). `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test`, `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild lint-types` and `pixi run -e pyforge-guild platform-ci-local -- --test` are green
**Status:** done

### Story 87.5: The herald webhook forward survives a client disconnect and proves AC 6 in any order

As the operator who turned the forward dark again after Story 87.4's independent review failed,
I want the host's body read to stop on a client disconnect, the forward to leave Story 87.1's answers alone while it is inactive, and the tests to prove the forward through the host's real entry point in any order,
So that dev can turn the forward back ON, and herald 19.2's proof can run, only after a separate review has passed.

**Type:** fix • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** S-87.4 • **FR/AD:** `spec-pyforge-steward` CAP-68 and CAP-69 (both from `spec-mcp-era-isolation`), the CAPs Stories 87.3 and 87.4 bound; pap:AD-2 • Dream 2026-10-10 (herald forward disconnect) • operator ruling 2026-10-10 "Dark again + mint 87.5 (Recommended)" • landing held for an independent review (marshal Story 83.18: `landing_review: required` in the spec, dispatched with `--hold-landing`) • gates herald Story 19.2's live proof in place of 87.4 (cross-station; herald holds the gate as its own `blocked` row)
**Flag:** none of its own (a fix, spec-feature-flag-governance Q1); it turns 87.3's `pyforge.steward.herald_webhook_sidecar` back ON in dev, last, and leaves staging and production OFF
**Surface:** `src/shared/packages/django-pyforge/src/django_pyforge/sidecar_forward.py` (`_read_webhook_body` `:74`-`:95`, `dispatch_herald_webhook_forward` `:112`-`:132`), `src/platform/mcp_host/app.py` (`_dispatch_herald_webhook` `:43`-`:66`), `src/platform/tests/` (`test_webhook_herald_forward_host.py`, `test_herald_webhook_sidecar.py`, `test_openfeature_file_flags.py`, new tests), `src/platform/config/flag-overlays.json`, `src/shared/packages/pyforge-core/tests/unit/test_flags.py`, 87.3's spec (`flag.default`, its **Flag** bullet and one Spec Change Log line only), `docs/explanation/platform-deployment-architecture.md`, `docs/governance/flag-inventory/` (through its tool)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-87-5-the-herald-webhook-forward-survives-a-client-disconnect-and-proves-ac-6-in-any-order.md`
**Given** `main`, where 87.4 is `done` (merge `cd32c401cc`) and its spec's Review Triage Log records "Independent review of the landed merge `cd32c401cc`: FAIL (1 high, 1 medium, 3 low)", and `pyforge.steward.herald_webhook_sidecar` is OFF in dev again by the same day's hygiene change. `_read_webhook_body` `continue`s on every message that is not `http.request`, so a partial body followed by `http.disconnect` spins forever (100,001 `receive()` calls in the reproduction). The two flag-OFF tests of `test_webhook_herald_forward_host.py` share herald's cached `webhook_host._application` and fail run alone. With the flag OFF or the URL unset, a path under the webhook prefix that is not on the allowlist gets the host's generic 404 before the flag and URL are read. The sidecar answers 500 when `exc.name` is `pyforge`. The forward-ON tests build the ASGI scope by hand and never assert the forwarded headers or the sidecar's answer
**When** a client disconnects mid-body, the forward is inactive, the sidecar's env lacks `pyforge`, or the forward-ON tests run
**Then** `_read_webhook_body` stops on `http.disconnect` (and on any message that is not `http.request`) without contacting the sidecar and without spinning, and a test of a partial body then a disconnect asserts a bounded number of `receive()` calls and no sidecar request. With the flag OFF or the URL unset, every path under the prefix keeps Story 87.1's behaviour (the in-process herald or 87.1's 404 with the absent reason); the allowlist 404 applies only when the forward is active. The sidecar answers 404 when `exc.name` is `pyforge.herald`, its parent `pyforge`, or a module under `pyforge.herald.`, and re-raises any other. The forward-ON tests drive `config.asgi.application` through `httpx.ASGITransport` and assert that the three allowlisted headers reach the sidecar unchanged and the sidecar's status and JSON body reach the caller
**And** the AC 6 tests reset `webhook_host._application` in a fixture and pass run alone, in reverse order (`pytest -p no:randomly` with the node ids reversed) and in the full suite. Only after those pass, in the same commit set, the overlay, both flag-default tables and 87.3's flag block and change log read dev ON again (staging and production OFF), `test_herald_webhook_sidecar_flag_two_states` stays green, and `flag-gate-check` passes. Restoring the `continue`, dropping the cache reset, moving the allowlist check back before the flag and URL, or narrowing the absent check fails a new test (mutation). `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test`, `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild lint-types` and `pixi run -e pyforge-guild platform-ci-local -- --test` are green. The landing stops at a draft PR until an independent review passes
**Status:** done

## Epic 88: The preflight runs each task once, after its dependencies (fix under CAP-159)

Minted 2026-10-10 from the station Dream's entry of the same date (preflight deps run twice) and the operator's ruling
"Mint both (Recommended)". `pr-preflight` runs a task's `depends-on` both as separate lanes and again inside the task's
own `pixi run`, all at once, so the pages lanes collide in one `docs-site/` and one `dist/`, and the red lane cancels
the rest of the run. A defect against CAP-159 (Epic 71), so no CAP is minted; a new epic because Epic 71 is `done`.
**HARD boundaries:** the dependency graph is read from `pixi.toml` only (AD-1); every task keeps its own lane, exit
code and journal row (AD-8); lanes with no order between them stay concurrent; the lanes CI's rules select do not
change; `pr-preflight`'s command line, the `pre-push` hook and `PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SKIP` do not change.

### Story 88.1: The preflight runs each task once, after its dependencies

As the operator whose herald-sidecar pushes lost the whole preflight to a `pages-check` collision,
I want each task in a preflight to run once, after its dependencies are green,
So that a lane never rebuilds what another lane is building, and a red dependency is named once.

**Type:** fix • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-159 (FR-32; Stories 71.1, 71.2, 71.3, 71.10, 71.11); AD-1, AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-10 (preflight deps run twice) • operator ruling 2026-10-10 "Mint both (Recommended)"
**Flag:** none (a fix, spec-feature-flag-governance Q1)
**Surface:** `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/preflight.py` (`_expand_task` about :96-:117, the default lane argv at about :438, the pool at about :700-:745), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/preflight_ci.py` (only if a selected lane's dependency must be pulled in), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_preflight.py`, `test_preflight_concurrency.py`, `test_preflight_selection.py`
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-88-1-the-preflight-runs-each-task-once-after-its-dependencies.md`
**Given** `5f141b983e`, where `_expand_task` turns `pages-check`'s `depends-on` into the lanes `docs-site-install`, `docs-site-sidebar` and `pages-build` and keeps `pages-check` as a lane too, every lane starts with no order between them, and each runs `pixi run --frozen -e <env> <task>`, which re-runs the task's dependencies. On the herald-sidecar pushes (runs `ced93a76…` and `fdba8d13…`, head `71f555eeb5`) the four pages lanes started together, and `pages-check` failed with "download size mismatch" and then "assemble_pages: collision at herald/"; each red cancelled `detectors-ci` and two doctor lanes, and `pixi run --frozen -e site pages-check` alone exits 0
**When** the preflight runs
**Then** every task with a `cmd` is one lane that carries its dependencies from `pixi.toml`. A lane starts only after its dependencies finish `ok` and runs as `pixi run --frozen --skip-deps -e <env> <task>`, so within one preflight no task runs twice, and the journal holds one row per task. Lanes with no order between them stay concurrent
**And** a red dependency is the only lane reported red. Its dependents never start: they are journaled `cancelled` (`cancelled_by` the dependency), or `not-run` with `blocked_by` under `--keep-going`. Selection is unchanged: `PAGES_CHECK_LEAVES` stays the same four lanes, selected only when `docsite-check.yml`'s paths fire (Story 71.2), with a new assertion on their dependency edges. Dropping `--skip-deps` or the dependency wait fails the new tests (mutation); `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test` and `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild lint-types` green
**Status:** done

## Epic 89: A killed `platform-ci-local` run never blocks the next one (fix under CAP-152)

Minted 2026-10-10 from the station Dream's entry of the same date (platform-ci-local lock outlives its run) and the
operator's ruling "yes mint the platform-ci-local lock fix story". Story 63.7's machine-wide lock is held on a file
descriptor that every command the script starts inherits, PostgreSQL and Redis included. Both detach into a session of
their own, so when a run is killed they outlive it and keep the lock. The next run then waits its full 7200 s bound for
nobody and exits 2. A defect against CAP-152 (Story 63.7, Epic 63), so no CAP is minted; a new epic because Epic 63 is
`done`. **HARD boundaries:** the fix lives in `scripts/platform-ci-local.sh`, and marshal's verification command, its
kill path and its gate do not change; the lock stays one machine-wide `flock`, held by the run's own shell; a run never
signals, stops or reuses a process it cannot prove a dead run started; Story 63.7's overrides, stage flags, step names,
summary and exit codes stay.

### Story 89.1: A killed `platform-ci-local` run never blocks the next one

As the operator whose steward 87.3 re-verification waited two hours on a lock that a dead run's PostgreSQL and Redis still held,
I want a killed `platform-ci-local` run to free its lock when it dies, and the next run to clear what it left behind,
So that one killed verification never costs the next one its verdict.

**Type:** fix • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-152 (Stories 63.6, 63.7); AD-1 • Dream 2026-10-10 (platform-ci-local lock outlives its run) • operator ruling 2026-10-10 "yes mint the platform-ci-local lock fix story"
**Flag:** none (a fix, spec-feature-flag-governance Q1)
**Surface:** `scripts/platform-ci-local.sh` (the header's Overlap paragraph about :22-:25, `acquire_run_lock` about :57-:81, `start_services`, `stop_services` and the EXIT trap about :117-:139, the main body about :264-:273), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_platform_ci_local_concurrency.py` (only free ports in the shared helper and the SIGKILL test, and stubs that write the real tools' pid files; no assertion weakened), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_platform_ci_local_lock_recovery.py` (new)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-89-1-a-killed-platform-ci-local-run-never-blocks-the-next-one.md`
**Given** `8ad2d18d69`, where `acquire_run_lock` holds the `flock` on fd 9 with no close-on-exec, and `pg_ctl` and `redis-server --daemonize yes` inherit it and each lead a session of their own. On 2026-10-10 steward 87.3's verify-fix turn hit its 900 s cap (MRS-DISP-059, run `pyforge-steward-20261010T154144466Z-88c00f58`), and the run inside it died without its EXIT trap stopping the services. `.lock.holder` named the dead pid 2564298, `fuser` listed only the orphaned postmaster and redis-server, and the re-verification waited 7200 s and exited 2 with "lock held longer than 7200s"
**When** a run is killed (SIGTERM or SIGKILL, to its process group or to its shell alone) and another run starts
**Then** the lock was held by the dead run's shell alone, so the next run takes it without waiting out the bound. No service, step or helper the script starts holds the lock's descriptor. The `stop_services` call at the top of `start_services` stays the reclaim of what a dead run left in the shared work dir: PostgreSQL through `pg_ctl ... stop`, Redis through the pid file it wrote, each only after `/proc/<pid>/cmdline` proves the pid is that service, each stop logged by pid and waited for. It never signals a pid whose identity does not match. It refuses a port still held by anything it cannot prove: exit 2, no service started, nothing signalled, and the port, pid and command line named
**And** a wait that times out names the processes that hold the lock now. A `.holder` record whose pid is dead is shown as stale, not as the holder. A normal run and a SIGTERMed run still stop their own services through the EXIT trap. Story 63.7's six tests stay green, changed only to use free ports and stubs that write the real tools' pid files. New Docker-free tests run the real script with stand-ins that detach the way `pg_ctl` and `redis-server` do, and each fix fails them when removed (mutation). `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test` and `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild lint-types` are green
**Status:** done

## Epic 90: A pytest the preflight starts never inherits another run's basetemp (fix under CAP-159)

Minted 2026-10-10 from the station Dream's entry of the same date (preflight basetemp inherited) and the operator's
ruling "yes mint the steward preflight fix story". `pr-preflight` gives each lane its own pytest basetemp and cache dir
by appending them to whatever `PYTEST_ADDOPTS` it inherited. The pytest runs that steward's own preflight tests start,
in scratch repos under `tmp_path`, inherit the lane's `--basetemp`, which is a parent of their working directory, so
pytest refuses it and exits 4. Under `pr-preflight` steward's suite fails nine tests that pass in CI. A defect against
CAP-159 (Stories 71.3, 71.8, 71.9), so no CAP is minted; a new epic because Epics 71 and 88 are `done`. **HARD
boundaries:** the fix lives in steward's preflight modules and steward's tests; which lanes run, what each runs, the
journal, the exit codes, `pr-preflight`'s command line, the `pre-push` hook and `PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SKIP` do not change;
every pytest option the invoker set, other than a basetemp or a cache dir, still reaches each lane; no other station's
code changes.

I want every pytest the preflight starts to carry only its own basetemp and cache dir,
So that steward's suite gives the same verdict under `pr-preflight` as in CI, and no test passes on a usage error.

**Flag:** none (a fix, spec-feature-flag-governance Q1)

**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-90-1-a-pytest-the-preflight-starts-never-inherits-another-run-s-basetemp.md`
**Given** `a0f47b7301`, where `_lane_scratch_env` appends the lane's `--basetemp` and `-o cache_dir` to the inherited `PYTEST_ADDOPTS` unchanged, `collect_pytest_node_ids` runs `pytest --collect-only` with the inherited environment, and the reduced-lane test helper hands its child pytest a raw copy of the environment. On hygiene PR #2092's `--keep-going` preflight (run `089d593c`, 28 lanes) `pyforge-steward-test` failed 9 of 2294 tests with "basetemp must not be empty, the current working directory or any parent directory of it", "config source: via PYTEST_ADDOPTS"; the same 32 tests pass with `PYTEST_ADDOPTS` unset and fail the same 9 with `PYTEST_ADDOPTS=--basetemp=<dir>`
**When** a lane, the node-id collection or a steward test starts a pytest while `PYTEST_ADDOPTS` carries another run's `--basetemp` or cache dir
**Then** the child pytest carries only its own: the lane's env keeps the inherited options except any `--basetemp` and any `cache_dir` override, in every spelling pytest accepts, then adds the lane's own once; the collection run drops the same two and adds none; the reduced-lane test helper builds its env with `_lane_scratch_env`, as the pool does; the other inherited options pass through in order
**And** with an outer `--basetemp` set to a parent of the tests' scratch repos, the nine tests pass, changed only through the reduced-lane helper's env, and a new test proves the lane env, the collection and a real child pytest under it; `test_collect_pytest_node_ids_raises_on_failure` and the scratch mutation test assert the failure they exist for, never a usage error (exit 4); the spec records every test in the ten `pyforge-*` packages and `src/platform` that starts a child pytest, and whether it inherits `PYTEST_ADDOPTS`; Stories 71.x and 88.1's lane selection, suite reduction, dependency order and journal are unchanged; restoring the inherited basetemp fails the new test (mutation); `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test` and `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild lint-types` green
**Status:** done

## Epic 91: The host lists its station portals at `/stations/` (feature under unifying CAP-3)

Minted 2026-10-11 from the station Dream's entry of that date (stations index) and the operator's ruling "Keep 37.1;
mint steward index (Recommended)", confirmed the same day with "lets do this", which asks for a flag dark in production
first, a Dream entry and the story's own review. Herald Story 37.1 keeps `/stations/` at its with-herald answer, a 404
from the Lane 1 catch-all, because nothing is mounted at the prefix. This epic gives the prefix a page: the station
portals a request may reach, each mounted or absent with the reason Story 87.1 records. A new page is a feature under
`spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy` CAP-3, with CAP-10's record, CAP-12's roles and CAP-13's flag, so no CAP is minted. It
opens a new epic because Epic 87 holds the host's boot fixes. **HARD boundaries:**
- `src/platform/` never imports `pyforge.*` (pap:AD-2). The index imports no station portal module and no
  `config.station_api` at module scope.
- With `pyforge.steward.stations_index` OFF, `/stations/` answers exactly as it does today. Staging and production stay
  OFF.
- Read-only: no model, migration, dependency, setting or environment variable. `django-pyforge` and every portal
  package are untouched.
- Herald's code, specs and ledger are herald's. The story waits on herald 37.1 as its own `blocked` row.

### Story 91.1: The stations index lists each station portal as mounted or absent, and why

As the operator running the platform host, with or without every station's package installed,
I want `/stations/` to list each station portal I may reach, mounted with its link or absent with its reason,
So that one page says what the host is running and why something is missing.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** — (cross-station gate: herald `37-1-the-herald-portal-loads-without-herald-and-says-why`; minted `blocked`, the operator flips it once 37.1 is `done` on `main`) • **FR/AD:** `spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy` CAP-3 (Eight portals, one session), with CAP-10, CAP-12 and CAP-13; pap:AD-2; canopy:AD-11 • Dream 2026-10-11 (stations index) • operator ruling 2026-10-11 "Keep 37.1; mint steward index (Recommended)" • landing held for an independent review (marshal Story 83.18: `landing_review: required` in the spec, dispatched with `--hold-landing`)
**Flag:** `pyforge.steward.stations_index` (openfeature-file, global; production off, staging off, dev on; fallback: `/stations/` falls through to the Lane 1 catch-all as today; cleanup 90 days after ON everywhere)
**Surface:** `src/platform/config/stations_index.py` (new), `src/platform/config/urls.py` (one `path` just before the `stations/` include at `:40`), `src/platform/platformapp/templates/stations/index.html` (new), `src/platform/config/flags.json` and `flag-overlays.json`, `src/platform/tests/test_stations_index.py` (new), `src/platform/tests/test_openfeature_file_flags.py` (`_SHIPPED_BOOLEANS`), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-core/tests/unit/test_flags.py`, `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/meta/test_stations_index_boundaries.py` (new), `docs/tutorials/local-platform-development.md` (`:59`), `docs/governance/flag-inventory/` (through its tool)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-91-1-the-stations-index-lists-each-station-portal-as-mounted-or-absent-and-why.md`
**Given** `baf4779b6d`, where `config/urls.py:40` includes `django_pyforge.urls` under `stations/` and that URLconf mounts only one `<station_name>/` include per discovered `PortalConfig` (`django_pyforge/urls.py:14`-`:17`), so `/stations/` falls to Wagtail's catch-all and answers 404. Story 87.1's `config.optional_components.absent_reason` records why herald is missing, but only the station-API 404 reads it, and only a process that imported `config.asgi` holds the record
**When** a request reaches `/stations/` with `pyforge.steward.stations_index` ON
**Then** a request with no station role, anonymous included, gets the 403 `require_station_role` gives. Any other request gets 200 and one row per discovered portal that `is_switcher_tile` admits for its roles, by station name. Each row is mounted, with a link to `/stations/<name>/`, or absent, with exactly 87.1's recorded reason and no link. The view loads `config.station_api` by module name on its first ON request, so the record exists whatever the entry point. On a host without herald, once herald 37.1 has landed, the herald row reads absent and the page answers 200
**And** with the flag OFF, `GET` and `OPTIONS /stations/` answer byte-identically to a URLconf without the index route, a Wagtail page published at that slug included, and nothing new is imported or logged. No response carries 87.1's remedy, a traceback or a path. The request writes nothing, and the diff adds no dependency or migration. A steward meta-test reads `stations_index.py` and `urls.py` with `ast` and reds a module-scope import of `pyforge.*`, a station portal package or `config.station_api`. The flag is registered in its four places, a two-state test covers it, and `flag-gate-check` passes. The local-platform tutorial names the page and its flag. Moving the flag check after the role check, answering OFF with a bare 404, answering 200 with no role, skipping the `config.station_api` load or rendering the remedy each fails a test (mutation). `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test`, `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild lint-types` and `pixi run -e pyforge-guild platform-ci-local -- --test` are green. Every Spec `spec-surface-check` names gets a memlog entry and one scoped stamp, and the landing stops at a draft PR until an independent review passes
**Status:** done (herald 37.1 landed on `main` 2026-10-11, PR #2134; flipped from `blocked` under the operator's 2026-10-11 pre-authorization "Queued after 37.1")

## Epic 92: The unsplit-Redis eviction guard proves broker loss on every run (fix under unifying CAP-11)

Minted 2026-10-11 from the station Dream's entry of that date (eviction guard flake) and the operator's ruling "Mint
steward fix story (Recommended)". Story 49.5's regression guard runs cache-eviction pressure on one shared Redis and
asserts the broker loses state, which proves the split survival test is not vacuous. Redis's `allkeys-lru` evicts from a
sample, so the guard's single fill sometimes leaves both broker keys standing: 8 of 60 runs failed on `27af54f73b`, and
the guard refused warden 16.1's and herald 37.1's landings on 2026-10-11. A defect against `spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy`
CAP-11 (Story 49.5, Epic 49), so no CAP is minted; a new epic because Epic 49 is `done`. **HARD boundaries:** test-only,
in `src/platform/tests/test_redis_cache_eviction_broker_survival.py`; the chart's Redis split, its eviction policies, the
platform's Redis settings and `platform-ci-local` do not change; the split test and its helpers do not change; the guard
is never skipped, marked `xfail` or rerun.

### Story 92.1: The unsplit-Redis eviction guard proves broker loss on every run

As the operator whose platform-touching landings were refused by a guard that fails about one run in eight,
I want the unsplit-Redis guard to keep applying cache pressure, a bounded number of times, until it sees the broker lose state,
So that it passes on every run while still proving the split survival test is not vacuous.

**Type:** fix • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** `spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy` CAP-11 (Story 49.5; Story 40.2's split) • Dream 2026-10-11 (eviction guard flake) • operator ruling 2026-10-11 "Mint steward fix story (Recommended)"
**Flag:** none (a fix, spec-feature-flag-governance Q1)
**Surface:** `src/platform/tests/test_redis_cache_eviction_broker_survival.py` (the guard `test_unsplit_redis_loses_broker_state_under_cache_pressure` about :271-:303, a new bounded-rounds helper beside `_fill_cache_to_eviction` about :144-:175, and one new module constant)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-92-1-the-unsplit-redis-eviction-guard-proves-broker-loss-on-every-run.md`
**Given** `27af54f73b`, where the guard starts one `redis-server` at 4 MB with `allkeys-lru`, seeds a queued task, a stream entry and a PEL row, runs `_fill_cache_to_eviction` once (50 KB values until `evicted_keys` moves, then 50 more writes) and asserts the broker lost something. Warden 16.1's landing verification (run `pyforge-warden-20261011T180723612Z-6ef4d113`) failed `platform-ci-local -- --test` on it (MRS-GATE-015, "1 failed, 1224 passed, 14 skipped"), herald 37.1's failed the same way, and 8 of 60 runs of the guard alone failed with "unsplit redis must lose broker-side state under cache eviction; otherwise the split survival test would pass vacuously"
**When** the guard runs
**Then** after the 49.5 fill it writes turnover rounds (`2 * maxmemory // _FILL_VALUE_BYTES` fresh keys each, under a round-unique name), reads the broker after each and stops at the first round that shows loss, at most `_BROKER_LOSS_MAX_ROUNDS` (20); the shared instance stays `allkeys-lru`, and `broker_lost` keeps its four conditions and its message, which also names the rounds run and `evicted_keys` when it fails
**And** the guard passes 20 of 20 consecutive runs of its node id, and the whole module 20 of 20; with the eviction pressure removed the guard fails 5 of 5 runs (mutation); the split test, its helpers and the module constants keep every line they have and the split test passes every run; the diff adds no skip, `xfail` or rerun; only the test file changes; `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test` and `pixi run -e pyforge-guild platform-ci-local -- --test` are green, and the `spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy` memlog reconciles the test file before one scoped stamp
**Status:** done

**And** with the fixture removed the OIDC test errors at teardown, and a deliberately leaking probe test errors while the test after it sees the baseline (mutations, reverted); the guard passes the whole suite as it stands (a full-suite survey found this test the only one that changes structlog's configuration); no skip, `xfail` or rerun; only the two files change; `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test` and `pixi run -e pyforge-guild platform-ci-local -- --test` are green, and the `spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy` memlog reconciles both files before one scoped stamp

**And** the 3.12 residue in `src/platform` and the lines that call the targets `py312` are corrected; removing the portal glob, removing the platform check, hard-coding `py314` or dropping the `requires-python` floor each fails a named test (mutation); `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test`, `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild lint-types`, `pixi run -e pyforge-guild platform-ci-local -- --test` and `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild pyforge-station-tests` are green, and every Spec `spec-surface-check` names gets a memlog entry and one scoped stamp
**Status:** done

## Epic 96: `platform-ci-local` never runs a stage on a stale env (fix under CAP-152)

Minted 2026-10-11 from the station Dream's entry of that date (platform-ci-local syncs a stale env) and the operator's
ask "Do this task here: Make platform-ci-local sync a stale env". `scripts/platform-ci-local.sh` takes its tools from the
checkout's `platform-ci-test`, `platform-dev` and `pyforge-warden` envs, and installs one only when its `bin/python` is
missing. A worktree whose envs predate a `pixi.lock` change merged from `main` therefore runs its stages, and marshal's
cross-surface check, on the old envs; steward 91.1's verification failed 15 platform tests that way on 2026-10-11. A
defect against CAP-152 (Stories 63.6, 63.7, 89.1), so no CAP is minted; a new epic because Epics 63 and 89 are `done`.
**HARD boundaries:**
- The fix lives in `scripts/platform-ci-local.sh` and steward's tests. Marshal's command, `pixi.toml` and `pixi.lock` do
  not change.
- `pixi install --frozen` only: never `pixi update`, `pixi add`, `pixi lock`, `--locked` or an install that may write
  `pixi.lock`.
- Stories 63.7's and 89.1's lock, reclaim, port refusal, stage flags, step names, overrides and exit codes stay. Their
  tests keep every test function and assertion; only the shared stub-root helper gains a `pixi` stand-in.

### Story 96.1: `platform-ci-local` never runs a stage on a stale env

As the operator whose story verifications run `platform-ci-local` in worktrees that merge `main` while the story is in flight,
I want every run to sync each env its stages use to the checkout's `pixi.lock` before anything starts,
So that a verdict is never about an env the lock has left behind.

**Type:** fix • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-152 (Stories 63.6, 63.7, 89.1); AD-1; AD-5 • Dream 2026-10-11 (platform-ci-local syncs a stale env) • operator ask 2026-10-11 "Do this task here: Make platform-ci-local sync a stale env"
**Flag:** none (a fix, spec-feature-flag-governance Q1)
**Surface:** `scripts/platform-ci-local.sh` (`ensure_envs` about :244-:250, the main body about :382-:386, the header's tools sentence about :30-:32), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_platform_ci_local_concurrency.py` (only `_prepare_stub_root`: a recording `pixi` stand-in in the stub `bin/`), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_platform_ci_local_env_sync.py` (new)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-96-1-platform-ci-local-never-runs-a-stage-on-a-stale-env.md`
**Given** `485d325d56`, where `ensure_envs` runs `pixi install --frozen -e <env>` for `platform-ci-test`, `platform-dev` and `pyforge-warden` only when `.pixi/envs/<env>/bin/python` is missing, whatever stages run, and `pixi run -e pyforge-guild` syncs only the Guild's env. On 2026-10-11 steward 91.1's worktree created `platform-ci-test` at 18:53 and merged `main` at 20:22 (`548b976af3`, carrying warden 16.1's `pyforge-warden` dependency for that env). Its next `platform-ci-local -- --test` failed 15 tests in `test_warden_fleet_inventory.py` with "No module named 'pyforge.warden'" until a hand-run `pixi install --frozen -e platform-ci-test`
**When** a run starts, with any set of stages
**Then** after it takes the lock and before any service or stage starts, it runs `pixi install --frozen --manifest-path "$ROOT/pixi.toml" -e <env>` once for each env its stages use (`test`: `platform-ci-test`, `platform-dev`; `images`: none; `container`: `platform-dev`; `promotion`: `platform-ci-test`, `pyforge-warden`), with the lock's descriptor closed and each install's output in a log under the work dir. For each env it prints `env <name>: installed`, `synced` or `current`, judged from pixi's own `conda-meta/pixi` record before and after, and adds the same as an `INFO` row to the summary
**And** a failed install exits 2, naming the env and pixi's exit code and showing the tail of its log, before any service starts or stage runs. The script calls `pixi` only as `pixi install --frozen`, and a test reads it to prove that. A new Docker-free test runs the real script with a recording `pixi` stand-in and proves each of these, and it fails when the old `-x ... ||` guard is restored (mutation). Stories 63.7's and 89.1's tests pass, with only their shared helper gaining that stand-in. `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test`, `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild lint-types` and `pixi run -e pyforge-guild platform-ci-local -- --test` are green, and the `spec-pyforge-steward` memlog reconciles every changed governed path before one scoped stamp
**Status:** done

## Epic 99: Every CI surface the operator can pause is declared once, switched by one convention, visible in one place and judged (spec-pyforge-steward CAP-166)

Minted 2026-10-12 from the station Dream's entry of that date (ci pause control plane), its plan
`docs/intake/ci-pause-control-plane/PLAN-2026-10-12.md`, and the operator's rulings Q1–Q8 (all the plan's recommended
answers). Two repository variables paused three jobs, about twenty-five jobs had no switch, six workflows sat disabled
in the GitHub UI with no recorded reason, `preflight_ci` could not read a `vars.*` term, and nothing listed or judged
any of it; the Pages deploy stayed stuck in `waiting` from 2026-10-06 to 2026-10-12 unseen. CAP-166 / FR-39. **HARD
boundaries:**
- Every outward write (`gh variable set|delete`, enabling or disabling a workflow) runs only through `steward ci
  pause|resume --yes`, operator-invoked; no story's tests or verify commands make a live write.
- No job loses its existing `if:` condition: the rendered fragment is a conjunction in front of it, and
  `workflow_dispatch` ignores every pause.
- The six existing variable names stay (ruling Q7); no required-status-checks ruleset is added (ruling Q6); runtime
  feature flags and the recipe-build workflows do not change.
- Steward reaches `gh` by subprocess (AD-1) and owns its exit codes (AD-8). Doctor, Herald and Marshal read `steward
  ci status --json` from their own chains and never import `pyforge.steward`.

### Story 99.1: The CI surfaces are declared once and the workflows are judged against them

As the operator who found two pause switches documented only in comments beside their `if:`,
I want one tracked registry naming every CI surface and its switch, and a check that holds the workflows to it,
So that no pause exists that the registry does not name.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-166 (FR-39); AD-1; AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-12 (ci pause control plane)
**Flag:** flag-exempt `detector-or-gate` (a registry check; a gated gate reports a silent green, spec-feature-flag-governance Q2)
**Surface:** `docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml`, `docs/governance/ci-surfaces.schema.json`, `scripts/ci_surface_gates.py`, its tests and fixtures under `tests/scripts/`, `scripts/detectors.py` (one repo-scope entry), `pixi.toml` (`ci-surface-gates-check`), `docs/reference/detectors.md`
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-99-1-the-ci-surfaces-are-declared-once-and-the-workflows-are-judged-against-them.md`
**Given** `938380474e`, where `PAUSE_CONTAINER_BUILDS`, `PAUSE_PLATFORM_CONTAINER_BUILDS` and four `PLATFORM_CI_*` opt-ins gate seven jobs and are documented only in workflow comments
**When** the registry is seeded with every active workflow, every job that reads a `vars.*` switch today, each `workflow_dispatch`-only workflow (switch `null`), the six `disabled_manually` workflows, and today's state (`paused_since` 2026-10-04 and 2026-10-09, `review_by` 2026-11-01 per ruling Q8)
**Then** `scripts/ci_surface_gates.py --check` exits 0 on the tree and exits 1 naming the workflow, job and switch on each planted fixture: a `vars.X` no row names, a row whose declared switch its job's `if:` does not read, a name outside the convention and its grandfather list (ruling Q7), a comparison other than the literal `'true'`
**And** `--check --live` (authenticated `gh`) also reds a `disabled_manually` workflow without a `paused-manually` or `retired` row and exits 2 when it cannot observe (unauthenticated, or a 0/60 rate limit), and only the offline mode runs in `detectors-ci`; the schema rejects a row missing `owner`, `state` or `cost`, and a `paused` row missing `paused_since`, `reason` or `review_by`.
**Status:** done

### Story 99.2: Every CI job carries its pause switch

As the operator who could pause three jobs out of about thirty,
I want every push, pull-request and scheduled job to read the master, workflow and (for an expensive job) job switch
in front of its own condition,
So that one variable stops any surface, and a manual dispatch still runs it.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** S-99.1 • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-166 (FR-39); AD-1; AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-12 (ci pause control plane)
**Flag:** flag-exempt `detector-or-gate` (the gate sweep and its completeness rule; a gated gate reports a silent green)
**Surface:** `.github/workflows/*.yml` (job `if:` lines and their comments only), `docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml`, `scripts/ci_surface_gates.py` (`--render`) and its tests, `pyforge/steward/preflight_ci.py` and its tests
**Given** the Story 99.1 registry and checker
**When** `--render` writes each job's fragment, `github.event_name == 'workflow_dispatch' || (vars.PAUSE_ALL_CI != 'true' && vars.PAUSE_<WORKFLOW> != 'true' [&& vars.PAUSE_<WORKFLOW>_<JOB> != 'true'] && (<existing condition>))`, with the job-level term only on a `cost: high` row (ruling Q2) and the six grandfathered names kept where they gate today
**Then** every push, pull-request and scheduled job in every active workflow carries its fragment with its existing condition unchanged inside it; `--check` gains the completeness rule (an active job with no row, or a row with no fragment, is a finding) and exits 0; `actionlint` and the workflow meta-tests (`test_workflow_path_filters_match.py`, `test_conformance_lane_wired.py`, `test_testing_kit_lane_wired.py`) stay green
**And** `preflight_ci` resolves each `vars.X` to the registry's declared state (a `paused` row reads `'true'`; any other row and an unregistered name read `''`, GitHub's value for an unset variable), so no site turns `unknown` because of a `vars.*` term and lane selection over the 2026-10-12 fixture diffs is identical before and after the sweep; a fixture with `PAUSE_ALL_CI=true` evaluates every job to skip except under `workflow_dispatch`.
**Status:** done

### Story 99.3: The operator sees the pause state of every CI surface in one command

As the operator whose Pages deploy sat stuck for six days with nothing reporting it,
I want one command that shows, per CI surface, whether it is live, paused, drifted, stuck or overdue, and why,
So that what is off, since when and until when is one read, and Doctor, Herald and Marshal read the same answer.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** S-99.1 • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-166 (FR-39); AD-1; AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-12 (ci pause control plane)
**Flag:** `pyforge.steward.ci_pause_control` (new; minted by this story, off by default, on in dev)
**Surface:** the steward package (a `ci` duty: `status`, `vars`), a shipped `ci-status-schema.json` under `src/pyforge/steward/data/`, `src/platform/config/flags.json`, `src/platform/config/flag-overlays.json`, `docs/governance/flag-inventory/pyforge-steward.md`, `.gitignore` (`.steward/ci-vars.json`)
**Given** the registry and a recorded `gh` fixture: variables, each workflow's state, its last 20 runs, and pending deployments
**When** `pyforge steward ci status` runs with the flag on
**Then** it prints one row per surface (switch, declared state, live value, `paused_since`, `review_by` with days left or overdue, owner, last green run) with one verdict from `live`, `paused`, `opt-in-off`, `paused-manually`, `retired`, `drift`, `stuck`, `overdue`: a replay of the 2026-10-06 waiting deploy reads `stuck`, five consecutive cancelled runs read `stuck`, a live value the registry declares otherwise reads `drift`, a `disabled_manually` workflow with no row reads `drift` naming it, and an elapsed `review_by` reads `overdue`
**And** `--json` validates against the shipped, frozen schema (`$id: urn:local-recipes:pyforge-steward:ci-status-schema`, additive changes only); `ci vars --refresh` writes the gitignored `.steward/ci-vars.json` with `fetched_at`; an unauthenticated `gh` or a 0/60 rate limit refuses with a documented exit code (AD-8), never a green table; with the flag off the duty refuses naming the flag; the CLI duty-count assertions move with the new duty.
**Status:** done

### Story 99.4: The operator pauses and resumes a CI surface through one journaled path

As the operator who set two pauses by hand with no reason or review date on record,
I want to pause and resume a surface through one command that records why and until when,
So that every pause carries its reason, its owner and its review date, and nothing else writes one.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** S-99.3 • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-166 (FR-39); AD-1; AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-12 (ci pause control plane)
**Flag:** `pyforge.steward.ci_pause_control` (Story 99.3's)
**Surface:** the steward package (`ci pause`, `ci resume`), `docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml` (written by the duty, landed through a PR), `.gitignore` (`.steward/ci-pauses.log` if untracked)
**Given** the flag on and a registry row
**When** `pyforge steward ci pause <surface|switch> --reason "…" --review-by <date>` runs without `--yes`
**Then** it prints the `gh variable set` argv, the registry diff and the team-memory capture text, and makes no `gh` call and no write (a fake-subprocess test asserts both)
**And** with `--yes` it runs `gh variable set <SWITCH> --body true --repo rxm7706/local-recipes`, updates the row (`state`, `paused_since`, `paused_by`, `reason`, `review_by`) in the current checkout and says the row lands through a PR, appends `.steward/ci-pauses.log`, and runs `scribe capture --type project`; `resume` reverses it; a `gate: true` row refuses without `--i-am-removing-a-merge-gate` (ruling Q5) and a `retired` row refuses, both before any write; no test calls a live `gh`.
**Status:** done

### Story 99.5: The preflight honors what CI will skip, and says so

As an author whose local pre-push gate cannot see which CI jobs are paused,
I want `pr-preflight` to skip what CI will skip, say so before it runs, and let me override it,
So that the local twin judges a branch the way CI will, and a paused surface is never silent.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** S-99.2, S-99.3 • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-166 (FR-39); AD-1; AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-12 (ci pause control plane)
**Flag:** `pyforge.steward.ci_pause_control` (Story 99.3's)
**Surface:** `pyforge/steward/preflight_ci.py`, `pyforge/steward/preflight.py` and their tests, `scripts/pre_push_preflight.sh`, `scripts/platform-ci-local.sh`, `scripts/guild_image_ci.sh`
**Given** Story 99.2's declared-state resolver and a `.steward/ci-vars.json` snapshot
**When** `pr-preflight` selects lanes with the flag on
**Then** a snapshot no older than 24 h wins over the declared state, a stale or absent one falls back to it, and the banner names the source used; each lane skipped for a pause is journaled `skipped: paused in CI (<SWITCH> since <date>, review_by <date>)`; a banner before the run lists every paused lane; `--ignore-ci-pauses` runs them and journals that it did (ruling Q3)
**And** the `pre-push` hook prints the same banner; `platform-ci-local` and `pyforge-steward-guild-image-build` print it for their paused CI twin and still run every requested stage; with the flag off, selection is Story 99.2's declared-state resolution with no snapshot and no banner.
**Status:** done

### Story 99.6: A paused expensive build still runs once a week

As the operator whose paused Containerfiles rot unseen until the day the pause lifts,
I want each paused expensive build to run once a week on its own schedule,
So that a pause saves runner time without hiding a broken image.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** S-99.2, S-99.3 • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-166 (FR-39); AD-1; AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-12 (ci pause control plane)
**Flag:** flag-exempt `detector-or-gate` (a scheduled proof that a paused image still builds; a gated canary reports a silent green)
**Surface:** `.github/workflows/ci-canary.yml`, `docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml` (`canary`), `scripts/ci_surface_gates.py` and its tests, the steward package (`ci status` reads the canary)
**Given** the registry's `paused` rows with `cost: high` (today the guild image and the platform container builds)
**When** `ci-canary.yml` runs on `schedule: 0 4 * * 0` or `workflow_dispatch`, gated by `PAUSE_CI_CANARY` like every workflow
**Then** it builds each such surface once through the pixi task its paused job runs (docker engine only) and writes each result to the job summary; it is a CI workflow, not a deploy, so AD-4 does not bind it
**And** `--check` reds a `cost: high` row without `canary`; `ci status` reads each paused row's last green canary run and reports `stuck` after 14 days without one; one green dispatch is recorded in the story spec.
**Status:** done

### Story 99.7: The hand-disabled workflows are retired or parked, and the convention is documented

As the operator with six workflows disabled in the GitHub UI since 2026-08-24 and no record of why,
I want the dead ones deleted, the parked ones recorded, and the pause convention written down once,
So that every off switch in the repo has a reason and a reader knows where to look.

**Type:** chore • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** S-99.1, S-99.4 • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-166 (FR-39); AD-1; AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-12 (ci pause control plane)

**Surface:** `.github/workflows/staged-recipes-linter.yml`, `linter_issue_comment.yml`, `reusable-staged-recipes-linter.yml`, `reusable-staged-recipes-linter-selftest.yml`, `.github/workflows/scripts/**`, `docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml`, `docs/how-to/pause-ci.md`, `docs/map.yaml`, `AGENTS.md`
**Given** six workflows `disabled_manually` since 2026-08-24 12:06–12:07 with no recorded reason (the Spec's open question)
**When** the story lands
**Then** the four staged-recipes-linter workflows and the `.github/workflows/scripts/` files only they use are deleted, each with a `retired` row (ruling Q4); `test-all` and `kedro-viz-publish` carry `paused-manually` rows with the operator's recorded reason and `review_by` 2026-11-01; `--check --live` exits 0
**And** `docs/how-to/pause-ci.md`, registered in `docs/map.yaml`, documents the convention, `steward ci status|pause|resume` and the dispatch carve-out; `AGENTS.md` § *Running and verifying* gains one pointer line (scribe's parity meta-test and `governance-currency` stay green); no tracked doc outside a dated log entry cites `ACTIONS_ENABLED` as current state.

### Story 99.8: A red main run on a CI surface reaches the next preflight

As the operator landing a branch while a workflow that gates no PR is failing on `main`,
I want `steward ci status` to report each surface's latest `main` run and `pr-preflight` to say which ones are red,
So that the next landing knows `main` is broken before it merges on top.

**Type:** fix • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-166 (FR-39; "visible in one place") • reopens Epic 99 (doctor Story 41.5) • Dream 2026-10-25 (a red main run reaches no landing) • operator rulings 2026-10-25 ("Red-main banner (Recommended)", "Fix, no flag (Recommended)") • retro-pyforge-atlas-2026-10-25 candidate fix 3
**Flag:** none (a fix, spec-feature-flag-governance Q1)
**Surface:** `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/ci.py` (`_fetch_runs`, `_build_rows`, `run_status`), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/data/ci-status-schema.json` (one additive row property), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/preflight_ci.py` and `preflight.py` (the advisory banner), `.gitignore` (`.steward/ci-status.json`), `docs/how-to/pause-ci.md`, unit tests under `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/` and the checked-in `tests/fixtures/ci_status_master_on.json`
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-99-8-a-red-main-run-on-a-ci-surface-reaches-the-next-preflight.md`
**Given** `529f06d52b`, where `ci status` reads each workflow's last 20 runs but reports only `last_green_run`, and `pr-preflight` shows pauses but nothing of `main`'s state
**When** `steward ci status` has run in the last 24 hours and a registered surface's newest completed `main` run did not succeed
**Then** that row's `latest_main_run` names the conclusion, time and run id, the envelope is saved to `.steward/ci-status.json`, and `pr-preflight` prints one advisory line per red run, naming every surface that shares it
**And** the envelope still validates against the schema (fixtures included); a missing, stale or unreadable snapshot prints one line and nothing else; the verdict and exit code never change; `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test` green
**Status:** done

## Epic 100: The estate's library and BMAD pins move on one policy-driven, measured, repeatable path (spec-pyforge-steward CAP-167)

Minted 2026-10-13 from the station Dream's entry of that date (estate refresh) and its plan
`docs/intake/estate-refresh/PLAN-2026-10-13.md`, whose recommended answers Q1–Q9 are the capability's assumptions
until the operator overrides them. The operator's six-step hand sequence (`pixi upgrade --pinning-strategy latest-up`
with 28 `--exclude` flags copied from a `pixi.toml` comment, then `pixi update`, then `pixi install -a`) left a batch on
`main` that lost five caps, two channel tables and two floors, would red `llms-full-check` (81), `bmad-estate-check` and
the liquibase channel-policy test, left `environment.yaml` stale, and moved the `bmad-method` floor to 6.12.2 while the
installed core stayed 6.12.0. CAP-167 / FR-40. **HARD boundaries:**
- Steward's own code never writes `pixi.toml`: `pixi upgrade` and the repo mutator `scripts/pin_policy_restore.py` do,
  as subprocesses (AD-1; AD-5 as read since Story 72.1). Every derived surface regenerates through its own generator,
  never a regex over a derived file.
- `pixi update` runs only under `estate refresh --transitive`, operator-invoked; agents run `estate plan`, and
  `refresh` without it inside a worktree that lands through a PR. `--apply-bmad`, `suite advance` and `anaconda upload`
  stay the operator's.
- A lost cap, table or floor is restored from its policy clause, never from taste; an unsolvable restore is a refusal
  naming the row. PostgreSQL stays 17 and the libpq caps stay (fnd:CAP-12).
- No daemon, workflow, cron, Dependabot or Renovate; `plan --due` is the only clock. Steward reaches `pixi`, `git`,
  `gh` and every generator by subprocess (AD-1) and owns its exit codes (AD-8). Doctor keeps its currency ledgers and
  drift warnings on its own chain; no story here writes a doctor surface.
- Installer-owned trees (`_bmad/**`, the bmad and skf skills, the caveman skill and hooks, `.claude/settings.json`) are
  re-applied only through their own installers under `estate realize --yes`, operator-invoked; a probe never writes
  (ruling Q10). No package-level station pin is rewritten: a locked version outside a station's range is refused and
  named, and the move is that station's story (ruling Q13).
- Every environment and every station: the verify tier is the Guild, core, testing-kit, the eight station envs,
  `platform-ci-test` and `pyforge-foundry-full`, `--all` adds the rest, and every row carries its platform; other
  platforms are lock-solved and said so (rulings Q12, Q14). A-side, ledger mode `rebuild`; no B surface.

### Story 100.1: The pin policy is declared once and an upgrade is judged against it

As the operator whose "do not move" list is a 28-exclude comment in three generations,
I want one tracked policy naming every held package with its clause, reason, authority and retirement condition, and a
check that holds `pixi.toml` and any change to it against that policy,
So that a lost cap, a flattened channel table or a lowered floor is a red finding, not a line in a 21,000-line diff.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-167 (FR-40); AD-1; AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-13 (estate refresh)
**Flag:** flag-exempt `detector-or-gate` (a registry and its check; a gated gate reports a silent green, spec-feature-flag-governance Q2)
**Surface:** `docs/governance/pin-policy.yaml`, `docs/governance/pin-policy.schema.json`, `scripts/pin_policy_check.py`, `scripts/pin_policy_restore.py`, their tests and fixtures under `tests/scripts/`, `scripts/detectors.py` (one repo-scope entry), `pixi.toml` (the `pin-policy-check` task in the Guild tasks feature; the three comment lines at `:45-48` replaced by one pointer line; no pin moves), `docs/reference/detectors.md`
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-100-1-the-pin-policy-is-declared-once-and-an-upgrade-is-judged-against-it.md`
**Given** `233e07909b`, where the policy is `pixi.toml:45-48` and the 2026-10-13 batch (plan § 2) lost five caps, two tables and two floors
**When** the registry is seeded with every package the comment excludes as a `never-move` row with its reason and authority (`python`, `nodejs`, `pixi`, the `django` / `channels` / `daphne` / `asgiref` / `channels_redis` / `wagtail` / `coderedcms` family, `postgresql` / `psycopg` / `psycopg2` / `libpq` / `pgvector` under fnd:CAP-12, the `conda-build` / `conda-index` / `conda-libmamba-solver` / `conda-forge-ci-setup` / `conda-forge-pinning` family, `grayskull`, `conda-recipe-manager`, `conda-smithy`, `shellcheck`, `frozendict`, `networkx`, `rattler-build-conda-compat`, `cachebox`), every declaration that carries a cap, an inline channel table or an exact pin as a `cap`, `channel` or `exact` row, and the defaults (`pinning_strategy: latest-up`, `floors_never_lower: true`)
**Then** `python scripts/pin_policy_check.py --check` exits 0 on the tree and exits 1 naming package, feature and clause on each planted fixture: a `<` cap with no row, a `{ channel = … }` table with no row, a row naming a package no feature declares, a pointer line that differs from `--render-command`; `--diff <base-ref>` over the 2026-10-13 batch (a fixture pair committed under `tests/scripts/fixtures/`) yields exactly nine findings — five lost caps, two flattened tables, two lowered floors — and zero on a floor-only move; a row with no `until` is a warning, never a red
**And** `--fan-out` reads every station package-level range (`src/shared/packages/*/pyproject.toml` and each package `pixi.toml` `[package.run-dependencies]`, through `llms_full_check.py`'s reader) and exits 1 naming the station and the file when the locked version falls outside a range (a fixture with `bmad-loop` 0.13.0 locked against marshal's `<0.13`), 0 otherwise; `scripts/pin_policy_restore.py --from <base-ref>` rewrites each named declaration to its policy clause at the new floor, keeps its comment, runs `pixi lock`, re-runs the check (exit 0), and refuses naming the row when the lock cannot solve (a fake-subprocess test asserts the refusal and that nothing was written); `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild pin-policy-check` exits 0; `scripts/detectors.py` lists it once among the repo-scope detectors so `detectors-ci` and `pr-preflight` run it; `docs/reference/detectors.md` matches its generator; no pin in `pixi.toml` moves.
**Status:** done

### Story 100.2: One command moves the library pins on the policy and restores what the upgrade loses

As the operator who runs six commands from memory,
I want `pyforge steward estate plan|refresh|status`,
So that a refresh is one command that renders its own upgrade, judges its own result and leaves a journal.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** S-100.1 • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-167 (FR-40); AD-1; AD-5; AD-7; AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-13 (estate refresh)
**Flag:** `pyforge.steward.estate_refresh` (new; minted by this story, off by default, on in dev)
**Surface:** the steward package (an `estate` duty: `plan`, `refresh`, `status`), a shipped `estate-journal-schema.json` under `src/pyforge/steward/data/`, `.steward/estate-refresh/` (tracked journals), `src/platform/config/flags.json`, `src/platform/config/flag-overlays.json`, `docs/governance/flag-inventory/pyforge-steward.md`, `docs/governance/guild-roster.json`, `.claude/hooks/pre-shell.py` and `.cursor/hooks.json` (the `steward-estate-transitive` denial row, ruling Q3)
**Given** Story 100.1's policy and check, the flag on, and a fake-subprocess `pixi`
**When** `pyforge steward estate plan` runs
**Then** it runs `pixi upgrade --dry-run --json` with the rendered excludes and prints one row per move (package, feature, from, to, held-by), the BMAD table (pinned floor, installed `_bmad/_config/manifest.yaml`, upstream latest through doctor's `bmad_method` reader, fail-open offline) and the days since the last journal; `--due` exits non-zero past 30 days and 0 otherwise; the plan writes nothing, so an agent may run it
**And** `estate refresh` refuses on a dirty tree or on `main` before any call; it renders the command from the policy, runs `pixi upgrade --pinning-strategy latest-up --json` with the rendered excludes, runs `pin_policy_check.py --diff` and `pin_policy_restore.py`, runs `pixi lock`, runs `bump-pixi-version` when the `pixi` floor moved, and writes `.steward/estate-refresh/<date>.json` validating against the shipped frozen schema (`$id: urn:local-recipes:pyforge-steward:estate-journal-schema`, additive changes only) with the base SHA, the platform, every move, every loss restored, the fan-out verdict, the `realize-due` register rows (Story 100.5's register, empty until it lands) and a `bmad` block (`pinned`, `installed`, `applied: false`); a red fan-out reds the refresh and the journal says so; `pixi update` runs only under `--transitive`, and the session hook denies `steward estate refresh --transitive` to agents through a new `session_denials` row whose reason names the operator form (the hook's matcher-equals-roster assertion stays green); `estate status` prints the last journal; with the flag off every verb refuses naming the flag; the CLI duty-count assertions move with the new duty; no test calls a live `pixi`.
**Status:** done

### Story 100.3: Every derived surface regenerates from the lock by its own generator

**And** it reads the Specs whose surface governs each moved file from `scripts/.spec-surface-baseline.json` (never a hard-coded list; seven for `pixi.toml` today), appends one `(event)` line per Spec through `_bmad/scripts/memlog.py append` naming the paths and the journal, runs `spec_surface_check.py --write-baseline --spec <project>/<spec>` for exactly those after `git add`, re-runs `spec-surface-check` (exit 0), and refuses naming the file on a memlog without frontmatter; `--fix-floors` over the 2026-10-13 batch fixture brings `llms-full-check` from 81 findings to 0 with the prose unchanged; no test calls a live generator that needs the network.

**And** one red environment, lane, fan-out or probe (`stale`, `missing`) reds the run; an installed `bmad-method` core below the pinned floor is `stale`; every row carries `platform`; the journal validates against the schema with the `verify` rows added; `estate status` prints, per platform, the lock it last confirmed; the Guild's cold install is recorded against CAP-5's 2 GB bound; a fake-subprocess fixture asserts the argv per environment, lane and probe, that the working tree is never installed into, and that a paused lane is journaled, not run; no test runs a live install.

### Story 100.6: The hand sequence is retired by one how-to and one pointer

As a reader of `pixi.toml`'s three comment generations and the PR-gate notes,
I want the hand sequence replaced by one how-to and one pointer,
So that the next refresh is read from one page and run as one command.

**Given** Stories 100.1–100.5 landed

**Then** `docs/how-to/refresh-the-estate.md`, registered in `docs/map.yaml`, documents the policy file and its hold classes, the realization register and its classes, the six verbs (`plan`, `refresh`, `realize`, `reconcile`, `verify`, `status`), the operator-only `--transitive` and `realize --yes`, the station pin fan-out, the verify tiers and the per-platform confirmation, the journal, the monthly cadence and the PR landing (`maintenance` label, the marshal merge subject); `AGENTS.md` § *Running and verifying* gains one pointer line and § *Policy*'s `pixi.toml` line names `estate reconcile` as the way `environment.yaml` regenerates (scribe's parity meta-test and `governance-currency` stay green); `docs/reference/agent-instruction-notes.md` § *PR CI gates* points at the how-to
**And** no tracked doc outside a dated log entry carries the 28-exclude command; `docs-map-render-test` and `docs-gen-test` green.
