## Epic 101: The `platform-ci-local` stubs leak no process, and the holder scan takes seconds (fix under CAP-152)

Minted 2026-10-14 from the station Dream's entry of that date (platform-ci-local stub leak) and the operator's approval
"the platform-ci-local test stubs leak processes, and the holder scan is slow". Story 63.7's stub `pg_ctl` and
`redis-server` record a bash wrapper's pid and leave its `sleep 999999` child running when that pid is stopped. On
2026-10-14 the host held 6,222 such orphans. With that many processes, the lock waiter's `readlink`-per-descriptor
holder scan took minutes, and Stories 63.7's and 89.1's lock-wait tests hit the 120 s subprocess timeout, which redded
steward 99.7's verification and marshal's pre-push preflight. A defect against CAP-152 (Stories 63.7, 89.1, 96.1), so
no CAP is minted. It is a new epic because Epics 63, 89 and 96 are `done`.
**HARD boundaries:**
- The fix lives in steward's three `test_platform_ci_local_*.py` files and in `scripts/platform-ci-local.sh`'s holder
  scan and contention report. Nothing else in the script changes.
- `PLATFORM_CI_LOCAL_LOCK_WAIT` and its 7200 s default, the exit codes, the contention report's format, the stage
  flags, the step names and the summary stay. No existing test function or assertion is weakened or deleted.
- No test or tool kills, counts or matches processes host-wide. Every check is scoped to the test's own marker.

### Story 101.1: The `platform-ci-local` stubs leak no process, and the holder scan takes seconds

As the operator whose host gathered 6,222 orphaned stub processes in a day of test runs,
I want every `platform-ci-local` test to stop everything it starts, and a lock wait to name its holders in seconds at any process count,
So that the suite neither fills the host nor times out on its own leftovers.

**Type:** fix • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-152 (Stories 63.7, 89.1, 96.1); AD-1 • Dream 2026-10-14 (platform-ci-local stub leak) • operator approval 2026-10-14 "the platform-ci-local test stubs leak processes, and the holder scan is slow"
**Flag:** none (a fix, spec-feature-flag-governance Q1)
**Surface:** `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_platform_ci_local_concurrency.py` (`_prepare_stub_root`'s two service stand-ins about :83-:89 and :112-:118, `_run_platform_ci_local`, the SIGKILL test's env, a marker helper, an autouse leak fixture and a regression test), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_platform_ci_local_killed_holder.py` (`_kill_and_follow`'s teardown, `lock_holder.sh`, the fixture import), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_platform_ci_local_env_sync.py` (the marker, the fixture import), `scripts/platform-ci-local.sh` (`acquire_run_lock` about :70-:74, `_report_lock_contention` about :83-:116, `_pids_holding_path` about :127-:143)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-101-1-the-platform-ci-local-stubs-leak-no-process-and-the-holder-scan-takes-seconds.md`
**Given** `e0ad43f18c`. The stub `pg_ctl` writes `$pgdata/postgres` as a bash script that runs `sleep 999999`, starts it with `setsid … &` and records `$!` in `postmaster.pid`; the stub `redis-server` does the same with `redis.pid`. `stop_services` kills the recorded wrapper and its `sleep` survives, so every stubbed run that starts services leaks two processes, and `lock_holder.sh` leaks a third. The two lock-wait tests (33.33 s and 2.89 s on a quiet host) left three. `_pids_holding_path` uses `fuser` when present (since `07fa34a20d`) and otherwise forks a `readlink` per descriptor of every process: 110.6 s at 6,549 processes, called twice per report. The waiter keeps fd 9 open while it scans, so it lists itself and its own helpers as holders
**When** the steward suite runs the three `platform-ci-local` test modules, or a run waits past `PLATFORM_CI_LOCAL_LOCK_WAIT`
**Then** each stub execs its long-running process (`exec -a "$0 $*" sleep 999999`), so the recorded pid is the process the stop path kills and its cmdline still passes `_is_our_postgres` / `_is_our_redis`; `lock_holder.sh` execs its `sleep`; the killed-run helper kills its holder's process group in teardown; every process a test starts carries `PLATFORM_CI_STUB_MARKER=<tmp_path>`, and an autouse fixture fails any test that leaves a process carrying that entry (killing only those); a named regression test runs one start/stop cycle and finds no marked process left
**And** the waiter closes fd 9 before it reports and scans once; `_pids_holding_path` keeps `fuser` and otherwise walks `/proc/<pid>/fd/*` with bash's own `-ef` test, forking nothing per descriptor, and prints one pid per line, sorted and unique; a test runs the function's fallback with `fuser` and `readlink` off `PATH` and gets exactly the holders' pids; the stale-record test asserts the report lists exactly its holder; each lock-wait test passes in under 60 s; reverting the stub `exec` fails the regression test and the fixture (mutation); `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test` is green and leaves no process carrying the suite's marker, and the `spec-pyforge-steward` memlog reconciles every changed governed path before one scoped stamp
**Status:** done

## Epic 102: The Pages deploy workflow is steward's duct (spec-pyforge-steward CAP-168)

Minted 2026-10-14 from the station Dream's Realization-log entry of 2026-10-13 (the Pages duct), the herald Dream's
same-day lead entry (the Pages deployment, redesigned; M2), the brainstorm and design-thinking records of 2026-10-13
and the operator rulings of that day, via `bmad-spec` (CAP-168, FR-41; AD-4 amended). Measured 2026-10-13: the Pages
workflow ran 1,342 times since 2026-09-13 on an unconditional push trigger — 720 deploys, 621 cancelled — with a
full-history checkout each time; a replay of the published-inputs filter deploys 132 times in the same 30 days; a
deploy sat in `waiting` from 2026-10-06 to 2026-10-12 unseen. The file sat on herald's surface while steward owned the
duty, the budget, the registry row and the preflight that reads it. **HARD boundaries:**
- Steward owns the duct: the workflow file, the registry rows, the `pages` duty, the budget. Herald keeps the
  artifact's content (`docsite/**`, `docs-site/**`, `pages-build`, `pages-check`, `/herald/`); atlas keeps the
  Kedro-Viz producer (Story 30.1); doctor keeps `map.yaml`. No story here edits `docsite/**` or `docs-site/**` beyond
  a file name.
- One `deploy-pages` caller (herald herald:AD-21); no bot commit on `main`; nothing outward beyond this repository's own
  Pages, release and Actions. Steward reaches `gh`, `git`, `curl` and the build tasks by subprocess (AD-1); `main()`
  keeps every exit code (AD-8); the duty-count assertions move with the duty (AD-7).
- No job loses its CAP-166 fragment; `workflow_dispatch` ignores every pause; no workflow step or test writes a
  repository variable (`steward ci pause|resume --yes` is the one write path). The registry rows under
  `docs/governance/` land with the story that needs them, as a governance act.
- Every file another Spec governs is a co-governor reconcile on that Spec's memlog and a scoped stamp; a `blocked`
  row is the operator's — cross-station dependencies are named in `Deps:`, never minted blocked.

### Story 102.1: The Pages workflow is steward's and fires only on the published inputs

As the operator paying for 720 deploys and 621 cancelled runs a month from a workflow named for a retired console,
I want the Pages workflow on steward's surface as `pages.yml`, firing only when a published input changes, with a
shallow sparse checkout,
So that a Dream or governance edit costs no build, no deploy and no cancelled run, and the duct has one owner.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-168 (FR-41), extending CAP-166, with CAP-159; AD-4 (amended 2026-10-14), AD-1; herald herald:AD-21 kept • Dream 2026-10-13 (the Pages duct) • operator rulings 2026-10-13
**Flag:** flag-exempt `detector-or-gate` (a workflow's `on:` block, checkout depth and sparse paths are evaluated by GitHub before any step can read a flag; the 99.2 / 99.6 precedent; the flag `pyforge.steward.pages_duty` is minted by 102.2)
**Surface:** `.github/workflows/dashboard.yml` → `.github/workflows/pages.yml` (a `git mv`; `name`, header, `on:`, the checkout), `docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml` (the three `dashboard*` rows' `workflow:` field), `scripts/pixi_version_registry.py` (the setup-pixi site), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/meta/test_invariants.py`, `tests/unit/test_preflight_selection.py` (a new case), every tracked file that names the old path as current (`docs/MAP.md`, `docs/dashboard/README.md`, `docs/explanation/enterprise-deployment.md`, `docs/how-to/README.md`, `docs/how-to/deploy-the-docs-site-to-github-enterprise-pages.md`, `docs/how-to/github-actions-recipe-ci.md`, `docs/how-to/presentation-deck.md`, `docs/reference/github-workflows.md`, `docs/governance/flag-inventory/pyforge-herald.md`, the comments of `docsite-check.yml`, `kedro-viz-publish.yml` and `platform-ci.yml`, `docsite/tools/pages_second_host.py`, `src/platform/tests/test_console_parity_homes.py`, `pyforge-herald`'s `webhook.py` and `test_pages_artifact.py` — each a co-governor reconcile), `spec-pyforge-steward` and `spec-pyforge-herald` memlogs (the surface move)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-102-1-the-pages-workflow-is-steward-s-and-fires-only-on-the-published-inputs.md`
**Given** `dashboard.yml` on herald's surface, `on: push: branches: [main]` with no filter, `fetch-depth: 0`, and the `dashboard`, `dashboard.build`, `dashboard.deploy` registry rows pointing at it
**When** the file becomes `.github/workflows/pages.yml` on steward's surface with `on.push.paths` exactly `docs/tutorials/**`, `docs/how-to/**`, `docs/reference/**`, `docs/explanation/**`, `docs/index.md`, `docs/404.md`, `docs/map.yaml`, `docs/dashboard/**`, `docs-site/**`, `docsite/**`, `presentations/**` and the workflow itself, `workflow_dispatch`, one weekly cron, `fetch-depth: 1` and a sparse checkout that admits `presentations/*/project/*.html` and nothing under `presentations/*/src/pptx`
**Then** a test parses the workflow and asserts the trigger, the cron and the absence of `pull_request`; `git log --follow` reaches the pre-rename history; the registry rows read `workflow: .github/workflows/pages.yml` with ids, switch and `stuck_rules` unchanged and `python scripts/ci_surface_gates.py --check` exits 0; `pixi-version-check` exits 0 with the `pages.yml setup-pixi` site; `pixi run --frozen -e site pages-check` exits 0 on a `--depth 1` sparse clone (recorded); `git grep 'workflows/dashboard.yml'` hits only dated log entries
**And** `test_preflight_selection.py` shows `pages.yml` firing `pages-check` on a `docs/tutorials/` edit and not on a `docs/dreams/` edit; the `spec-pyforge-steward` memlog reconciles `.github/workflows/pages.yml` and every co-governed path, the `spec-pyforge-herald` memlog records the file leaving its surface, each named Spec is stamped scoped and `spec-surface-check` exits 0; thirty days after the merge the operator reads under 150 deploys and no cancelled streak of five (recorded in the spec)
**Status:** done

### Story 102.2: One Pages workflow in upstream shape builds on every change and deploys only what changed

As the operator who wants the fleet board and guild status refreshed every six hours without six deploys a day,
I want one workflow in BMAD-METHOD `docs.yaml`'s shape — build on `pull_request` and `push`, deploy on `main` only when
the built tree changed — with the PR twin folded in and the registry rows renamed,

one filter drives one workflow.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** S-102.1 • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-168 (FR-41), extending CAP-166, with CAP-159; AD-4 (amended 2026-10-14), AD-1, AD-8; canopy:AD-11 • Dream 2026-10-13 (the Pages duct) • operator rulings 2026-10-13 (six-hourly schedule with the no-op skip)

**Surface:** `.github/workflows/pages.yml` (`pull_request`, the six-hourly cron, the `build.json` stamp step, the PR artifact upload, the deploy `if:`, concurrency, timeouts), `.github/workflows/docsite-check.yml` (deleted), `docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml` (`dashboard*` → `pages*`, `PAUSE_PAGES`, `docsite-check*` retired), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/pages.py` (new: `tree_hash`, `build_json`, `decide_deploy`, the flag read), `scripts/pages_build_stamp.py` (new), `scripts/pixi_version_registry.py` (the docsite-check site leaves), `tests/unit/test_preflight_selection.py` (the oracle moves to `pages.yml`), `tests/unit/test_ci_status.py` (a `pages` stuck case), `tests/unit/test_pages.py` and `test_pages_flag_states.py` (new), `src/platform/config/flags.json`, `src/platform/config/flag-overlays.json`, `docs/governance/flag-inventory/pyforge-steward.md`
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-102-2-one-pages-workflow-in-upstream-shape-builds-on-every-change-and-deploys-only-what-changed.md`
**Given** Story 102.1's `pages.yml` deploying every green build on `main`, `docsite-check.yml` as a separate PR twin with its own filter, and the ruling that the status pages refresh per deploy and every six hours
**When** `build` runs on `pull_request` and `push` with one filter and one six-hourly cron, runs `pages-check` and `docs-site-validate`, writes `build.json` (tree hash with `build.json` and the built-from footer excluded, built-from sha, run id, built at) and uploads the site as the Pages artifact on non-PR events and as the `pages-site` artifact on PRs; `deploy` runs only on `main` when `needs.build.outputs.changed == 'true'`, read by comparing the built hash with the live `<SITE_URL>/build.json` (absent reads as changed; flag off reads as changed); `docsite-check.yml` is deleted; the registry rows become `pages` (`PAUSE_PAGES`, `stuck_rules` kept), `pages.build`, `pages.deploy`, and `docsite-check*` retire

**And** `test_pages_flag_states.py` runs `decide_deploy` in both states with `flag_states`; one dispatched run after landing followed by a second with no tree change shows the second's deploy skipped (recorded); `pyforge-steward-test` is green and the memlog reconciles every governed path before one scoped stamp
**Status:** done

### Story 102.5: `configure-pages` is authoritative on every host and the artifact ships as a release asset

As the operator of the enterprise and air-gapped copies who today flips a herald flag and rebuilds behind a mirror,
I want the build to take its host from `actions/configure-pages` with no flag step, and each deploy from `main` to
publish the deployed bytes as `pages-site.zip` on a release,
So that every host serves the same reviewed artifact and the how-to shrinks to Settings → Pages → GitHub Actions and
a download.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** S-102.2; herald Story 42.3 (CAP-60, the second host needs no flag) (the reader of `pyforge.herald.pages_second_host` retires; the coordinator fills the key) • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-168 (FR-41); AD-1, AD-8, AD-9; herald herald:AD-21 (one artifact, N hosts); CAP-165 (protected refs, untouched) • Dream 2026-10-13 (the Pages duct) • operator ruling 2026-10-13 (`configure-pages` authoritative; the zip for the air-gapped host)
**Flag:** `pyforge.steward.pages_duty` (Story 102.2's; off, no release asset; the host-env drop is not gated — it removes a step)
**Surface:** `.github/workflows/pages.yml` (the `export_pages_host_env.py` line leaves the flags step; `SITE_URL` from `steps.pages.outputs.base_url`; the `deploy` job's `contents: write` and the asset step), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/pages.py` (the step's gating helper), `tests/unit/test_pages_release.py` and `test_pages_release_flag_states.py` (new), `docs/how-to/deploy-the-docs-site-to-github-enterprise-pages.md` (§ 4 and the Story 31.1 sentence go; *Pull the zip* added — a co-governor reconcile on `spec-pyforge-doctor`)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-102-5-configure-pages-is-authoritative-on-every-host-and-the-artifact-ships-as-a-release-asset.md`
**Given** a `Resolve Pages host env` step that hands `SITE_URL` to the build only when herald's flag is on, a how-to whose § 4 flips that flag on the enterprise copy, and an air-gapped host that rebuilds behind a mirror
**When** the `export_pages_host_env.py` line leaves the step, the `build` job sets `SITE_URL` from `configure-pages` on every event, the `deploy` job (`contents: write`, the only job with it) zips the build job's `pages-site` artifact with `build.json` inside and publishes it as `pages-site.zip` on a rolling `pages-site` pre-release re-pointed at the deploying commit after each `deploy-pages` success (gated on the flag and on non-PR events), and the how-to loses its flag section and gains *Pull the zip*
**Then** a workflow test finds no `export_pages_host_env.py` step, `SITE_URL` from `steps.pages.outputs.base_url`, `contents: write` on `deploy` only, and the asset step after `deploy-pages` with its two gates; the how-to has no `pages_second_host`, names `gh release download pages-site -p pages-site.zip` and `build.json`, and `docs-site-validate` exits 0; `pages-site` matches no protected-refs pattern and `protected_refs_ruleset_check.py` exits 0
**And** `test_pages_release_flag_states.py` runs the gating helper in both states; after one deploy from `main`, `gh release view pages-site` shows the deploying commit and one `pages-site.zip` listing `build.json` (recorded, with a second deploy moving the tag); `pyforge-steward-test` and `flag-gate-check` are green and the `spec-pyforge-doctor` memlog reconciles the how-to before its scoped stamp
**Status:** done

## Epic 103: Steward's CI, estate and environment reports say what is true (fixes under CAP-166, CAP-167, CAP-5 and CAP-159; one CAP-159 feature)

Minted 2026-10-14 from the station Dream's entry of that date (CI and estate follow-ups) and three open deferred-work
rows, each found by a story that landed that day: `DW-steward-101-1-1` (`steward ci status` has no row for the
`PAUSE_ALL_CI` master switch), `DW-steward-100-7-2` (`estate reconcile` commits whatever the network-dependent
bmad-suite generator writes) and `DW-doctor-47-1-1` (`pyforge-ci` is called stdlib-only, but `spec-kit` brings
`jsonschema` and `pyyaml`). The operator ruled the third 2026-10-14: "yes stdlib only". Each story is a defect against
a shipped CAP, so no CAP is minted. It is a new epic because Epics 63, 99 and 100 are `done`, and it is Epic 103
because Epic 102 (the Pages duct, CAP-168) was minted the same day.
Story 103.4 was added the same day, from the Dream entry's addition (ci-surface-gates fixture writes): the CAP-166
gate's tests write their cases into the tracked tree, so every `pr-preflight` since Story 102.1 leaves two tracked
files modified and the pre-push hook refuses the push. It touches no environment and no consumer.
Story 103.5 was added the same day, from the Dream entry's addition (preflight lane identity): `pr-preflight` keeps a
lane's result, log and scratch by its task name alone, so two lanes of one task in two environments overwrite each
other. It is a fix under CAP-159, whose Epics 71, 88, 90 and 98 are `done`; it changes no environment and no workflow.
Story 103.6 was added 2026-10-15, from the Dream entry's addition (CI arbitrates the deferred lanes), under the
operator's three rulings of that day (the `spec-pyforge-steward` memlog has them verbatim): a touched scope in which
`pr-preflight` runs only what the diff touches and leaves every other lane to GitHub CI, chosen by a new flag or an
environment variable, and a `verify-deferred` catch-up. It is the epic's one feature, behind the new flag
`pyforge.steward.preflight_ci_arbitrates`, and extends CAP-159 with no CAP; it changes no environment, no workflow
and no hook.

Story 103.7 was added 2026-10-15, from the Dream entry's addition (ci status retired rows), on the operator's ruling
"fix this now": `steward ci status` reads the run list of every row's workflow, retired rows included, and two of the
deleted files have no GitHub record, so every read is partial and every landing records its CI pauses as `unknown`. It
is a fix under CAP-166 and the one story in the epic that touches consumers, by the exception below.
**HARD boundaries:**
- No consumer station changes: doctor's, herald's and marshal's readers of `steward ci status --json` stay as they
  are, and the envelope changes only additively (one row, one optional row property, no new verdict). The one
  exception is Story 103.7 (operator ruling 2026-10-15): marshal's steward adapter and landing record, and doctor's
  `ci_pause_drift` source, a few lines each, to read a partial envelope from stdout; the envelope gains two optional
  top-level properties and no row property or verdict.
- The bmad-suite generator is conda-forge-expert's: its story invokes the skill before any edit and closes with the
  skill's retro (`SKILL.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, a semver bump), in a `retro(cfe):` commit.
- `pixi lock` only, never `pixi add` or `pixi update`; no environment other than `pyforge-ci` changes its packages.
- Nothing outward: no workflow variable, feedstock, upstream or other-repository write.

### Story 103.1: `steward ci status` reports the master switch, and every surface it pauses

As the operator reading doctor's findings, the dossier tile or a landing's PR body while `PAUSE_ALL_CI` is on,
I want the CI status report to carry the master switch and mark every surface it skips as paused,
So that none of the three readers says CI ran when it did not.

**Type:** fix • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-166 (Story 99.3); AD-1, AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-14 (CI and estate follow-ups) • closes `DW-steward-101-1-1`
**Flag:** none (a fix, spec-feature-flag-governance Q1)
**Surface:** `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/ci.py` (`_build_rows` about :565, `_status_payload` about :714), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/data/ci-status-schema.json` (one optional row property), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_ci_status.py`, `docs/how-to/pause-ci.md` (the **Master switch** paragraph)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-103-1-steward-ci-status-reports-the-master-switch-and-every-surface-it-pauses.md`
**Given** `846d8383f5`. `_build_rows` emits rows for registry surfaces and unregistered switches only, and counts `conventions.master` as registered, so `PAUSE_ALL_CI` never has a row; each verdict reads only the row's own switch, though every push, pull-request and schedule job reads `vars.PAUSE_ALL_CI != 'true'`. Doctor's `ci_pause_drift` WARNs only on `drift`, `stuck` and an overdue review; herald's tile lists every non-`live` row; marshal's landing rule lists a row only when its own `live_switch_value` is `true`
**When** `pyforge steward ci status --json` runs with the master unset, `"false"` or `"true"`
**Then** the payload has one `master:PAUSE_ALL_CI` row (declared `live`, the variable's live value, `live` while off, `drift` with a detail while on); every row whose jobs read the master, derived from the workflow files by `_ci_surface_gates_lib`'s parser, reads `paused` with a detail while it is on, below `drift`, `stuck` and `overdue`, and carries `paused_by_master`; drift is still judged on each row's own switch; the schema gains only the optional `paused_by_master` boolean and keeps its eight verdicts
**And** with the master off every other row is unchanged; the master-on payload, kept as a steward fixture its test regenerates, read by doctor's, herald's and marshal's own code in their own environments gives one doctor WARN naming the master, a tile listing the master and every gated row, and a marshal list containing `master:PAUSE_ALL_CI`, with no consumer file changed; `docs/how-to/pause-ci.md` says what the report shows; four mutations fail named tests; `pyforge-steward-test`, `pyforge-doctor-test`, `pyforge-herald-test` and `lint-types` are green, and each governing Spec's memlog reconciles the changed paths before its scoped stamp
**Status:** done

### Story 103.2: `estate reconcile` writes the bmad-suite floors from the batch, not the network

As the operator landing an estate refresh,
I want reconcile to set each bmad-suite floor from what the refresh moved and journal every floor with its source,
So that two reconciles of one batch write the same recipe, and no floor rises past what the estate builds or locks.

**Type:** fix • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-167 (Stories 100.3, 100.7); AD-1, AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-14 (CI and estate follow-ups) • closes `DW-steward-100-7-2`
**Flag:** none (a fix, spec-feature-flag-governance Q1)
**Surface:** `.claude/skills/conda-forge-expert/scripts/bmad_suite_metapackage.py` (`generate` about :331, `main` about :397, a batch mode and a JSON report), `.claude/skills/conda-forge-expert/tests/unit/test_bmad_suite_metapackage_rewrite.py`, `.claude/skills/conda-forge-expert/SKILL.md`, `.claude/skills/conda-forge-expert/CHANGELOG.md`, `.claude/skills/conda-forge-expert/MANIFEST.yaml`, `.claude/skills/conda-forge-expert/config/skill-config.yaml`, `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/estate_reconcile.py` (`_build_generators` about :98-:105, `run_reconcile` about :322), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_estate_reconcile.py`
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-103-2-estate-reconcile-writes-the-bmad-suite-floors-from-the-batch-not-the-network.md`
**Given** `846d8383f5`. The generator sets each member's floor to its upstream latest and silently falls back to the member recipe's version when a lookup fails; reconcile runs it whenever the batch moves a `bmad-*` package and journals only its exit code. Story 100.7's two reconciles on one branch wrote `bmad-loop >=0.13.2`, then `>=0.12.0`, while the lock held 0.12.0; main's `bmad-method >=6.12.2` and `bmad-module-skill-forge >=3.0.0` sit above their member recipes because the batch moved them
**When** `estate reconcile` runs `generate-bmad-suite` for a batch
**Then** it runs the generator in a new batch mode (`--journal <journal> --json`) that makes no upstream lookup: a member the batch moves gets the highest version the batch moved it to, any other member keeps the higher of its current floor and its member recipe's version, and no floor falls; reconcile writes the generator's per-member report (floor before and after, source, the move) to the journal and names each changed floor in its summary, and a generator failure or unreadable report fails reconcile with exit 1
**And** the generator's hand path, without the new flags, is byte-for-byte unchanged, and its `--json` names each fallback there; the implementer invokes the `conda-forge-expert` skill first, and the story closes with the skill's retro (`SKILL.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, a MINOR bump) in a `retro(cfe):` commit; four mutations fail named tests; `pyforge-steward-test` and the conda-forge-expert offline suite are green, no recipe under `recipes/` moves, and each governing Spec's memlog reconciles the changed paths before its scoped stamp
**Status:** done

As the maintainer of the repo-root scripts and their tests,
I want `pyforge-ci` to carry no runtime library, and every test that needs one to run where that library is declared,
So that a test built to skip there skips, nothing that ran stops running, and the six descriptions of the env are true.

**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-103-3-pyforge-ci-is-stdlib-only-as-its-tasks-say.md`
**Given** `846d8383f5`. `pyforge-ci` composes the `python` feature, whose `spec-kit` brings 37 packages (`jsonschema`, `pyyaml`, `pydantic`, `httpx`, …), and its own feature declares `ruamel.yaml`. In `pyforge-ci` `tests/scripts` passes 1554 tests; with the `spec-kit`-only packages blocked, 845 pass and 73 fail with 9 collection errors, each for want of `yaml`, across 32 files, and those 32 pass in `pyforge-guild`. Doctor's dispatch tests run in `pyforge-ci`, and its verdict-writer test skips there only by collection order
**When** `spec-kit` moves to its own feature, composed by the 13 other environments that compose `python`, and `ruamel.yaml` leaves the `pyforge-ci` feature
**Then** `pixi lock` changes only `pyforge-ci`'s packages (linux-64: 146 → 106) and `estate reconcile` regenerates `environment.yaml` and the generated docs; none of `yaml`, `jsonschema`, `ruamel.yaml`, `mcp`, `pydantic`, `httpx` or `openfeature` imports in `pyforge-ci`; each of the 32 files guards its libraries with a module-level `pytest.importorskip`, so `pyforge-doctor-scripts-test` has 0 failures and 0 errors; 31 of them run in a new `scripts-lib-test` task in `pyforge-guild`, a new `scripts-suite` step and `pr-preflight` leg, and the 32nd stays with the doctor aggregate step; Doctor's dispatch and verdict-writer tests skip in `pyforge-ci` in either collection order and run in `pyforge-doctor`
**And** every test node id that passed in `pyforge-ci` at `846d8383f5` passes in one of the job's three steps; the six descriptions, the env comments, the `scripts-suite` comments and `_doctor_dispatch`'s docstring are true against one definition of stdlib-only written in the `pyforge-ci` env comment, which names what the env still carries (the `python` feature's binaries, `gitpython`, `truststore`, the build, test and upload tools); a stdlib-only guard test pins the composition and the library split, and four mutations fail it; `pyforge-steward-test` and `pyforge-station-tests` are green, and each governing Spec's memlog reconciles the changed paths before its scoped stamp
**Status:** done

I want the CI-surface gate's tests to build every case they check in their own temporary directory,

Twelve of the file's 20 tests write workflow files, registries or workflow-state JSON into `tests/scripts/fixtures/ci_surface_gates/` in the tracked tree, and no test reads a file there that it did not write first. **Then** every test builds what it generates under its own `tmp_path`, and none writes into the repository tree; the 18 generated files leave git, because no test reads one it did not write and so none is a checked-in input; an autouse guard fails any test after which `git status --porcelain --untracked-files=all -- tests/scripts/fixtures/ci_surface_gates` is not empty, and a named test proves that the guard is active and that its status read reports both an untracked and a modified file in a scratch repository
**And** each test keeps its inputs, its detector arguments and every assertion; restoring one write into the repo tree fails the guard, and dropping untracked files from the status read fails the named test (mutations); `pyforge-doctor-scripts-test` (`pyforge-ci`), `pyforge-doctor-aggregate-scripts-test` and the file in `pyforge-guild` pass, `git status --porcelain` is empty after each and after `pr-preflight`, and the pre-push hook passes with no `PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SKIP`; `pixi.toml` and every import line stay as they are (Story 103.3's); the `spec-pyforge-steward` memlog reconciles the changed governed paths before one scoped stamp

### Story 103.6: A touched-scope preflight leaves the other lanes to CI, and `verify-deferred` catches up

As the operator pushing a wave of branches from a loaded host,
I want `pr-preflight` to run only what my diff touches and leave every other lane it would run to GitHub CI, chosen by a flag or an environment variable,
So that a push waits minutes rather than half an hour, and one command at the end verifies everything the pushes left to CI.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-159 (Stories 71.1, 71.2, 71.7, 99.5, 103.5); CAP-154 (the pre-push hook, unchanged); AD-1, AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-14 (CI and estate follow-ups; the 2026-10-15 addition, CI arbitrates the deferred lanes) • operator rulings 2026-10-15 (verbatim in the `spec-pyforge-steward` memlog)
**Flag:** `pyforge.steward.preflight_ci_arbitrates` (new, minted by this story; default off, on in dev, off in staging and production)
**Surface:** `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/preflight.py` (scope resolution, `run_preflight` about :764-:1137, the journal record about :1103-:1132, `main` about :1166), `preflight_ci.py` (the paths a lane's command names, the touched test), a new `preflight_verify.py` (the `preflight` duty and `verify-deferred`), `cli.py` (`DUTIES` about :49-:79, `_HELP`, `build_parser`, `resolve_duty`), their unit tests under `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/` (new `test_preflight_scope.py` and `test_preflight_verify_deferred.py`; the duty counts in `test_cli.py`, `test_restore_duty.py` and `test_estate.py`), `tests/scripts/test_lint_types_gate.py` (the hook's side; amended in implementation), `src/platform/config/flags.json`, `src/platform/config/flag-overlays.json`, `src/platform/tests/test_openfeature_file_flags.py`, `src/shared/packages/pyforge-core/tests/unit/test_flags.py`, `docs/governance/flag-inventory/pyforge-steward.md` (regenerated), `AGENTS.md` (§ Running and verifying)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-103-6-a-touched-scope-preflight-leaves-the-other-lanes-to-ci-and-verify-deferred-catches-up.md`
**Given** `fccf4f3ee7`. `run_preflight` runs every lane CI's own workflow rules select for the diff (Story 71.2), about 21 of 37 on a station branch and all of them on a shared-surface change, and `PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SKIP=1` (the hook's one opt-out) skips all of them. Under host load 29–114 on 2026-10-14/15 a run took 13–28 min and timed out (`chain_currency_sweep_check` past its 300 s limit, atlas's e2e port binds past 60 s), while GitHub runs the same lanes in about 10 min. Five planning detectors, `spec-surface-check`, `flag-gate-check`, `story-status-check`, `ledger-regression-check` and `chain-completeness-check`, are guild tasks of their own and took 0.6–2.9 s each at `f7be77efad`
**When** `pr-preflight` runs with `PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SCOPE=touched`, or with the variable unset while the flag reads on
**Then** of the lanes the selection and the CI pause filter leave, it runs a lane only when the diff (committed and working tree) changes its station package (`src/shared/packages/pyforge-<x>/` or `django-<x>/` for a lane in environment `pyforge-<x>`) or a path its own command line names, the five detectors in place of `detectors-ci` and in its environment, each lane those need, and every lane of the `lint-types` closure when a Python file under `src/` changed; every other lane is deferred, printed on stdout and journaled, and the run's journal record gains `mode` (`full` or `touched`) and `deferred` (each lane's `lane`, `environment` and `reason`, "CI arbitrates"); `PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SCOPE=full`, or the flag off with the variable unset, runs today's selection and journals `mode: full` with an empty `deferred`; any other value of the variable refuses with exit 2, never falling back, and an unreadable diff runs every selected lane and says so; `pyforge steward preflight verify-deferred [--since YYYY-MM-DD]` lists every journaled run since that date that deferred lanes and every push the skip log says skipped the preflight (branch, head, lanes, why), then runs `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild pr-preflight` with `PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SCOPE=full` on HEAD and exits 0 green, 1 red, 2 on a bad date and 3 when the preflight could not run
**And** `PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SKIP=1` keeps its meaning and its precedence (the hook skips before any scope is read, and passes the variable through when it runs); the hook, `pixi.toml` and every existing journal key stay as they are, and old records still parse; the flag sits in the tree with its metadata and overlay values, in the core and platform flag contract tests and in steward's flag inventory, and a test runs it in both states with `flag_states`; `AGENTS.md` names the flag, the variable and `verify-deferred` where it names the opt-out; four mutations fail named tests; `pyforge-steward-test`, the steward coverage gate (80% on each touched module), `pyforge-core-test`, the platform flag tests and `lint-types` are green, and each governing Spec's memlog reconciles the changed paths before its scoped stamp
**Status:** done

### Story 103.7: `steward ci status` reads no runs for a retired row, so a landing records the CI pauses

As the operator reading a landing's `CI paused at merge:` line, doctor's CI pause findings or the dossier tile,

**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-103-7-steward-ci-status-reads-no-runs-for-a-retired-row-so-a-landing-records-the-ci-pauses.md`
**Given** `f3cab009ec`. `pyforge steward ci status --json` exits 1 with stdout empty and its envelope on stderr. `_collect_snapshot` runs `gh run list` for every registry row's workflow, retired rows included; the retired `reusable-staged-recipes-linter` (`workflow_call`) and `reusable-staged-recipes-linter-selftest` (`workflow_dispatch` only), deleted by Story 99.7, never ran as workflows of their own, so GitHub has no record of them and both fetches 404. A failed fetch makes the read partial, `run_status` returns `ok=False`, and `main` prints a failed duty's summary on stderr. Marshal's adapter records `unknown` on any exit 1, as the doctor 48.1, herald 43.1 and atlas 30.1 landing journals of 2026-10-15 show; doctor parses stderr on exit 1; herald's tile reads a committed copy of stdout. The three `stuck` rows do not affect the exit code
**When** `ci status` runs against a registry whose retired rows name deleted workflow files, and against one whose live row's run list cannot be read
**Then** no run list is read for a retired row, which reads `last_green_run` null and can be neither partial nor `stuck`, so the first read exits 0 with the envelope on stdout; the second still exits 1, and with `--json` prints its envelope on stdout carrying `partial: true` and `run_list_errors`, the failed row's `last_green_run` `unknown`, while the table form keeps stderr; the schema gains only the two optional top-level properties, and a complete envelope, the master-on fixture included, is unchanged
**And** marshal's adapter records a read on exit 0 and on exit 1 with a valid envelope on stdout (adding `partial: true` to the record), and `unknown` on exit 1 without one; doctor reads a partial envelope from stdout and falls back to stderr, keeping its `ci-pause-partial` WARN; herald's tile validates a partial envelope unchanged; re-fetching retired rows and dropping the stdout request each fail a named test (mutations); the live command exits 0 with JSON on stdout after the fix; `pyforge-steward-test`, the steward coverage gate, `pyforge-marshal-test`, `pyforge-doctor-test`, `lint-types` and `flag-gate-check` are green, and each governing Spec's memlog reconciles the changed paths before its scoped stamp
**Status:** done

**When** the preflight runs two or more of those lanes
**Then** they share one mutex, `tree:docs-site`, so no two of them run at once, while a lane outside the set still overlaps them; a lane with a service key and the docs-site key takes both, in one sorted order, without deadlock; the pool never hands a slot to a lane whose mutex an in-flight lane holds, and a lane never holds a mutex while it waits for a dependency; the docs-site lanes trade turns among themselves, a docs-site lane that pending lanes wait on (`docs-site-install`) taking the first, so herald's built-site tests read this run's install, while every other lane keeps its declared position; after a red stop without `--keep-going` no lane starts (review and re-review, 2026-10-15)
**And** the set is one declared `frozenset` in `preflight.py`, and a steward meta test fails when a task that runs in `docs-site/`, depends on `docs-site-install`, runs a docs-site script that imports an npm package or spawns npm or astro, or runs herald's suite (whose tests call `astro_build`) is missing from it, or when it names any other task; the two validators that import only node builtins stay out; `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test` and `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild lint-types` green

  edit is Story 98.1's rule: `pyforge-station-tests.yml`'s steward job runs when `.gitignore`, which the new meta test
  reads, changes.

## Epic 107: The preflight refuses a stranded lane, and its serial reference resolves under pixi (fix under spec-pyforge-steward CAP-159; no CAP)

Minted 2026-10-16 from the station Dream's Realization log entry of the same date (a selected lane strands its
dependency). `run_preflight` never ends when a lane it runs depends on a lane outside the run: the lane is then never
ready and never blocked, and the scheduler loops with nothing in flight. CI's selection leaves a dependency out only
when nothing in CI runs it with the lane (a lane CI's reader matches by its command line, a fast-core detector, a lane
no CI step runs, and, found at re-review, a paused lane `--ignore-ci-pauses` re-adds without the dependency the same
pause holds back), so the run is refused, never widened. `pixi run -e pyforge-guild pr-preflight-lanes`, the serial
reference, fails before any lane starts, because pixi looks the `site` tasks' bare dependencies up in the environment
the run was invoked in. It closes `DW-steward-103-6-1`, `DW-steward-103-6-2` and `DW-steward-45-1-1`. It is a new epic
because Epic 106, the last CAP-159 fix, is `done`. *(Revised at review, 2026-10-16: the first cut pulled a missing
dependency in; the orchestrator's ruling dropped that for the refusal alone. The re-review added the
`--ignore-ci-pauses` case. The story spec's change log records why.)*
**HARD boundaries:**
- A fix: no CAP, no FR, no flag (`spec-feature-flag-governance` Q1), no `SPEC.md` change.
- The lanes CI's rules select, the journal and the exit codes do not change: a stranded run is refused with
  `EXIT_CONFIG` (2), the code every config refusal already uses, before any install or lane, and journals nothing. The
  run set is never widened beyond the selection, and the scheduler loop is not restructured.
- `pixi.toml` changes only the `depends-on` entries of the `[feature.site.tasks.*]` tasks, each now naming
  `environment = "site"`, and the rationale comment on herald's coverage gate: no dependency, version, lock or
  `environment.yaml` change, and `list_preflight_lanes` returns the same 36 lanes, in the same order with the same
  edges, before and after.
- The cross-station edits are herald's three meta tests that pin the `site` tasks' `depends-on` shape
  (`test_docs_site.py`, `test_docs_site_validators.py`, `test_one_generator.py`), restated to the new shape with no
  assertion weakened, and the docstring of herald's `test_the_coverage_gate_takes_no_install_dependency`
  (`test_built_site_tests_install_first.py`), whose stated reason this story changes.

### Story 107.1: A stranded preflight lane is refused, and pr-preflight-lanes resolves under pixi

As a contributor whose push runs `pr-preflight`,
I want a run that would strand a lane on a dependency it leaves out to stop at once and tell me why, and the serial
reference task to resolve under pixi,
So that the preflight never spins on a dependency nobody runs, never runs locally what CI does not, and
`pixi run -e pyforge-guild pr-preflight-lanes` runs the lanes it names.

**Type:** fix • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** fix under spec-pyforge-steward CAP-159 (FR-32; Stories 71.1, 71.2, 88.1, 99.5, 103.6); AD-1, AD-5, AD-8 • Dream 2026-10-16 (a selected lane strands its dependency) • closes `DW-steward-103-6-1`, `DW-steward-103-6-2`, `DW-steward-45-1-1` • orchestrating session's brief and review ruling, 2026-10-16
**Flag:** none — a fix (`spec-feature-flag-governance` Q1); the story spec records `flag-exempt: detector-or-gate`
**Surface:** `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/preflight.py` (`stranded_dependencies`, `stranded_lane_message`, `run_preflight`); `pixi.toml` (the `depends-on` entries of the five `[feature.site.tasks.*]` tasks that have one; the comment on `pyforge-herald-coverage-gate`); `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_preflight_stranded_lane.py` (new); `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/meta/test_pr_preflight_lanes_resolve_under_pixi.py` (new); `src/shared/packages/pyforge-herald/tests/meta/test_docs_site.py`, `test_docs_site_validators.py`, `test_one_generator.py` (the `site` tasks' `depends-on` restated), `test_built_site_tests_install_first.py` (one docstring); `planning-artifacts/deferred-work-ledger.md` (the three rows closed); `planning-artifacts/test-architecture.md` (regenerated by `bmad_tea_playwright.py`)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-107-1-a-stranded-preflight-lane-is-refused-and-pr-preflight-lanes-resolves-under-pixi.md`
**Given** `2ca62dbca0`, where `run_preflight` runs the selected lanes (less the paused ones, and in a touched run the cut and the fast core), and a lane whose dependency is not in the run is never ready and never blocked, so the scheduler loops with nothing in flight; and where `pixi run --as-is --dry-run -e pyforge-guild pr-preflight-lanes` exits 1 with "could not find the task 'pages-build'"
**When** the selection or a CI pause leaves out a dependency of a lane the run keeps, or a touched run's fast core depends on a deferred lane, or `--ignore-ci-pauses` re-adds a paused lane but not the dependency the same pause holds back, or pixi resolves `pr-preflight-lanes` from `pyforge-guild`
**Then** the run exits 2 before any install or lane, journaling nothing, and names on stderr each stranded lane, why it runs (its selection verdict's reason, the fast-core reason, or the pause of a lane `--ignore-ci-pauses` re-added), each missing dependency, that nothing in CI runs the lane with it, and the fixes (make the CI step run `pixi run <task>`, or add one where no step runs the lane, or drop the `depends-on`; for a fast-core detector, drop the `depends-on` or take the task out of the fast core; under `--ignore-ci-pauses`, name the dependency as its surface's local twin in `docs/governance/ci-surfaces.yaml`, or run without the flag, whose re-adds do not change); and pixi resolves all 36 tasks, because every `site` task's `depends-on` entry names `environment = "site"`
**And** when nothing is stranded, the run set, its order and the journal are what they were at `2ca62dbca0`; a steward meta test walks `pr-preflight-lanes` from `pyforge-guild` by pixi's lookup rule and fails naming every task it cannot find, and on the old `site` shape it fails first at `pages-build`, where pixi's dry-run stopped; `list_preflight_lanes` is unchanged; `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test` and `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild lint-types` green

## Epic 108: `estate reconcile` and `ci status` report what happened (fixes under spec-pyforge-steward CAP-167 and CAP-166; no CAP)

Minted 2026-10-17 from the station Dream's Realization log entry of the same date (reconcile's env matrix never
regenerates, and a retired row reads paused by the master). Two open deferred-work rows, each found by an independent
review that day outside its own story, show two steward reports saying less than what happened.
`DW-steward-103-3-2`: `estate reconcile`'s `env-matrix` generator passes the Dream positionally, so it exits 2, writes
nothing, and the run reads green, because only `generate-bmad-suite`'s exit is acted on.
`DW-steward-101-1-2`: `ci status` reads retirement from `kind`, but the registry records it in `state`, so a retired
row whose workflow still reads the master would carry `paused_by_master: true`.
Each is a defect against a shipped CAP, so no CAP is minted. It is a new epic because Epic 103, the last epic of
CAP-166 and CAP-167 fixes, is `done`. *(Revised at review, 2026-10-17: the BMAD-estate catalog generator ran with
`sys.executable`, the `pyforge-steward` env, which has no `pyforge.scribe`, so it always exited 1. Once a failed
generator reds the run, no reconcile could exit 0 in its own env. The orchestrator ruled it fixed inside this story:
the generator runs in `pyforge-guild`, as the checks do.)*
**HARD boundaries:**
- A fix: no CAP, no FR, no flag (`spec-feature-flag-governance` Q1), no `SPEC.md` change.
- In `estate reconcile`, these do not change: `generate-bmad-suite`'s early stop and report, the existing gating and
  warn checks and their order, the exit codes, and the spec-surface reconcile. The journal's `reconcile` block
  changes only additively: a `label` on every generator row, an `error` on a failed one, and one more check row.
- `scripts/pixi_env_matrix.py`'s interface does not change; only its usage docstring does.
- `ci status`'s envelope, verdicts and schema do not change, and no consumer of it (doctor, herald, marshal) changes.
- No test or verification runs a bare `pyforge steward estate reconcile`, which rewrites the latest tracked journal
  (`DW-steward-103-3-1`). The generator's argv is proved against a scratch copy of the script.
- The one workflow edit is Story 98.1's rule: `pyforge-station-tests.yml`'s steward job runs when
  `scripts/pixi_env_matrix.py`, which the new test reads, changes.

### Story 108.1: `estate reconcile` reds on a failed generator, and a retired CI row is never paused by the master

As the operator who lands an estate refresh or reads `steward ci status` while `PAUSE_ALL_CI` is on,
I want every reconcile generator to run as its script expects and any failure to red the run by name, and a retired
row never to read paused by the master,

**And** with every generator green, the run's summary and exit are what they were at `ab38be642f`, and its checks are those plus the one new row; a unit test runs the generator's and the check's argv against a scratch copy of the script (exit 0, the matrix written and current) and the old argv against it (exit 2, nothing written); every generator's argv is pinned as a literal list; each new rule's mutation fails a named test; `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-steward pyforge-steward-test` and `pixi run --frozen -e pyforge-guild lint-types` green; each governing Spec's memlog reconciles the changed paths before its scoped stamp

**And** 168 capability lines before and after; no id, order, provenance line or status moves; no Epic 44 key moves; `capability-ledger-check`, `chain-completeness`, `chain-currency` (steward) and `detectors-ci` stay green
**Status:** done

## Epic 112: The Platform CI `test` job's budget covers a slow mirror (spec-pyforge-steward CAP-174 / FR-48)

Minted 2026-10-26 from the station Dream's Realization log entry of the same date. The Platform CI `test` job was
cancelled at its 15-minute ceiling on 2026-10-23 and twice on 2026-10-26, two of the three on `main`'s own push runs.
Each time the `playwright install --with-deps chromium` step spent 636–668 s, nearly all of it an apt download at
38–43 kB/s that the runner image did not need. CAP-174 makes the job install only what the image lacks and sizes its budget from measured step times.
Epic 112 because draft PR #2274 claims Epic 109 (with steward capability ids 169-173 and FR-42..FR-47), Story 110.1 took 110 and
Story 111.1 took 111. **HARD boundaries:**
- The lane is never paused, disabled or set `continue-on-error`; its `platform-ci.test` registry row, its `if:`
  fragment and its suite do not change.
- `.github/actions/platform-test-setup`, the `platform-ci-test` environment and the other workflows' Chromium steps do
  not change.

### Story 112.1: The Platform CI `test` job installs only what its runner image lacks, and its budget covers a slow mirror

As the operator reading Platform CI on a PR or on `main`,
I want the `test` job to skip an apt download its runner image does not need and to carry a budget sized from what it does,
So that a slow apt mirror or runner reads as a slower green run, not a cancellation I learn to ignore.

**Type:** fix • **Effort:** S • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** FR-48 ← CAP-174; AD-1 (Playwright's own installer, no hand-kept apt list) • Dream 2026-10-26 (the Platform CI test job's budget is sized for a fast mirror)
**Flag:** none — a fix (`spec-feature-flag-governance` Q1); the story spec records `flag-exempt: detector-or-gate`
**Surface:** `.github/workflows/platform-ci.yml` (the `test` job's Chromium step and its `timeout-minutes`)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-112-1-the-platform-ci-test-job-installs-only-what-its-runner-image-lacks-and-its-budget-covers-a-slow-mirror.md`
**Given** the `test` job at `timeout-minutes: 15` running `python -m playwright install --with-deps chromium`, which took 636–668 s on runs 39925508594, 40306113110 and 40351088193 (attempt 1) while every other step summed to 365 s at worst over 178 green jobs
**When** the job runs on a PR or on `main`
**Then** it runs `python -m playwright install chromium`, launches the browser once, and runs `python -m playwright install-deps chromium` only when that launch fails, after a `::warning::` naming the fallback, then launches it again and exits the step after an `::error::` if it still does not launch; `timeout-minutes` is 25 and the comment beside it names 668 s, 365 s and 1,033 s
**And** the PR's own Platform CI `test` run, which installs the browser from scratch, completes inside 25 minutes with `test_playwright_records_zero_foreign_origin_requests` PASSED and no apt download in its log; `ci_surface_gates.py --check` and `detectors-ci` exit 0
**Status:** done

## Epic 114: Every pre-rule steward runtime capability sits behind a kill switch (8 flags, one per CAP; the Guild flag rule's retrofit capability, Q6)

Minted 2026-10-26 from the station Dream's entry of the same date and the operator's approval of the CAP-7 retrofit sheet (R1–R8): every capability the flag inventory reads as reachable at runtime and minted before the rule date sits behind one flag that defaults ON in every environment, a kill switch (spec-feature-flag-governance CAP-7; Q6 one flag per CAP; Q3 a flag-off verb stays listed and refuses with the usage code). Three shapes: a verb switch, an invariant switch (the dependent verb refuses, the defect never reopens; ruling R3) and a behaviour switch (the pre-rule behaviour at one read site). Each key carries its clock from 2026-10-26; Story 114.9 (backlog) retires them on 2027-01-24 (Q4; ruling R5). Rows: `spec-pyforge-steward` CAP-4, CAP-6, CAP-107, CAP-108, CAP-114, CAP-154, CAP-158; `spec-pyforge-unifying-strategy` CAP-6.
**HARD boundaries:**
- Keys are read only through `pyforge.core.flags` or `django_pyforge.flags`, spelled literally; one tree, no second reader.
- No station exit code changes: a refusal is the usage code 2.
- No done story spec is rewritten; the inventory is regenerated.

## Epic 116: The absorbed steward Spec folders live in one archive home (spec-one-chain-per-station:CAP-11)

Minted 2026-10-27 from `docs/governance/spec-one-chain-per-station/SPEC.md` (spec-one-chain-per-station:CAP-11; owner Dream
`docs/dreams/one-chain-per-station.md`, `owner: guild`) and that Dream's entry of the same date: everything the fleet retires
leaves the live tree for `archive/<original path>`, and after the archived Dreams (PR #2328) come the absorbed and archived
Spec folders, each with its memlog and its companions (CHAIN-STANDARD §11 step 2). On 2026-10-27, at `9dd0a8f1f9`, 35
`status: absorbed` Spec folders still sat under `_bmad-output/projects/pyforge-steward/planning-artifacts/specs/`, `docs/intake/` held two routed drops beside its README, and `docs/dreams/archive/` held the 2026-08-23 topology snapshot.
Steward mints no CAP and registers no FR for this story: the CAP is the Guild Spec's, enumerated here and in that
Spec's `.memlog.md`, the relay shape of doctor Epic 36, marshal Epic 75, herald Epic 33 and the 2026-10-26 mop. One of the
eight station stories of the 2026-10-27 one-PR close-out of spec-one-chain-per-station:CAP-11. **HARD boundaries:** every move is a `git mv`; nothing
is deleted; a moved file's text is unchanged except the memlog event appended before the move; a live companion is carried
into the station Spec folder, never archived (operator ruling 2026-10-27); dated records keep the path they were written
with; `SPEC.md` is never hand-edited (memlog event, then the anchor-asserting script render, both self-validate passes
logged). The story is a `chore`.

## Epic 117: The preflight and a PR's CI answer within a measured budget, and a station suite runs once (spec-pyforge-steward CAP-175 / FR-49)

Minted 2026-10-27 from the station Dream's Realization-log entry of the same date (the preflight answers in eleven minutes, a PR's CI in thirteen) and the operator's four rulings on it. Measured 2026-10-27 from every worktree's `.steward/preflight-runs.jsonl` (132 runs) and the Actions API: a full `pr-preflight` is 670 s at the median and 1950 s at worst (Epic 71 had it under a minute); one PR head is 12–13 min of wall and ~126 runner-minutes; `pyforge-steward-test` runs serial at 698 s (19 s on 2026-09-27); coverage gates re-run every touched suite; image builds cost ~1400 runner-s per PR; detectors run serially in CI; and five recent PRs each ran 8–10 of the 10 suite jobs because Story 98.1's arrays are what each suite's tests read. Rulings: (D1) image builds and the CFE regression net leave the PR path for push-to-main plus the nightly canary, the PR keeps the CFE `tests/meta`; (D2) coverage gates merge into the station test job and `coverage-gates.yml` retires; (D3) `preflight-budget` stays warn-only; (D4) fan-out shrinks by splitting live-tree readers from package suites, never by narrowing the arrays. Doctor (`detectors-ci`'s hygiene and chain-currency sources, `pyforge-doctor-test`'s pinned workers), herald, mason (`test-ci`), atlas, scribe and warden take their halves on their own chains and cite the story here they serve. **HARD boundaries:** a lane is skipped only where CI's own rule skips it; verdicts are read from exit codes, never through a pipe; `PYFORGE_PREFLIGHT_SKIP=1` stays the one journaled opt-out; the not-covered list (container, atlas's Chromium/DuckDB/WASM, herald's browser check, scribe's Postgres) does not grow; no test is deleted, skipped or re-marked to meet a budget; a `pixi.toml` edit regenerates `environment.yaml` in the same PR; the CFE suite task changes only through the `conda-forge-expert` skill and its retro; `SPEC.md` is never hand-edited; the station-tests arrays are never narrowed by hand (Story 98.1, rulings 2026-10-12); nothing is paused, disabled or set `continue-on-error`.

Amended 2026-10-27 (later the same day) from the Dream's second entry of the day — the wall is the lanes the waves did not touch (a shared-surface head 764 s and 94 runner-minutes after Waves 1–2; a memory-only head 347 s on an ungated image build; the repository is public, so minutes are free and the wall is the only cost): Stories 117.10–117.12 add sharded station jobs with an N-way gate merge, a shallow `changes` gate and shallow coverage diffs, and the platform suite under xdist. 117.6 and 117.8 (D1) come first in the build order.

### Story 117.1: The steward suite runs under pytest-xdist and its serial remainder alone

As the operator whose every push and every PR waits on these gates,
I want the steward suite runs under pytest-xdist and its serial remainder alone,
So that a green local run still means a green CI run, and both answer in minutes, not a quarter of an hour.

**Type:** chore • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-175 / FR-49 (extends CAP-159 and CAP-166; works with CAP-174, spec-pyforge-core:CAP-8, spec-coverage-gate-independence); lands on AD-1 (wrap, never reimplement: pytest-xdist, buildx, the registry render are the tools' own), AD-5 (provision wraps pixi) and AD-8 (exit-code sole ownership)
**Flag:** none (`type: chore`, `spec-feature-flag-governance` Q1)
**Surface:** `pixi.toml` (`[feature.pyforge-steward.dependencies]` gains `pytest-xdist`; task `pyforge-steward-test`), `pixi.lock`, `environment.yaml` (regenerated), `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests/unit/test_preflight_concurrency.py` (runs alone: it SIGINTs its own process), `@pytest.mark.xdist_group` on fixtures that share scratch, the six heaviest test files (`test_workspace_stacked_landing.py`, `test_cutover.py`, `test_estate_verify.py`, `test_estate.py`, `test_preflight_scratch.py`, `test_estate_reconcile.py`)
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-117-1-the-steward-suite-runs-under-pytest-xdist-and-its-serial-remainder-alone.md`
**Given** `pyforge-steward-test` is `pytest src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/tests -q`: 2501 tests in one process, 698 s at the median of the last week's full preflights (19 s on 2026-09-27) and 547 s in CI, with 19 real `git init` repos, ~60 `subprocess.run` calls and polling sleeps in the heaviest files
**When** the task runs `-n auto --dist loadgroup --ignore=…/test_preflight_concurrency.py && pytest …/test_preflight_concurrency.py -q` (the doctor pattern), fixtures that share a scratch path carry `@pytest.mark.xdist_group`, and the `--durations=40` offenders are fixed by class (one module-scoped `git init` template copied per test; in-process `cli.main(argv)` where a `pixi run` subprocess proves nothing extra, one true subprocess test kept per entry point; `threading.Event.wait` instead of polling sleeps)
**Then** the suite is green under xdist with the same collected count, the lane journals ≤ 240 s on 16 cores in a touched-scope preflight (measured 2026-10-27: ~120 s for 3056 tests under `--dist loadgroup`, 698 s before; the ≤ 70 s mark is Story 117.9's, the test-level fixes) and the `steward-test` CI job finishes ≤ 300 s on `ubuntu-latest`
**And** `environment.yaml` is regenerated in the same PR (`pyforge steward estate reconcile`), `pyforge-station-tests` runs the shared-surface fan-out green, and `preflight_xdist.lane_xdist_worker_map` now hands the lane its share
**Status:** done

**Then** every touched station's suite runs once per PR and once per preflight, the floor verdict for each station is byte-identical to the two-run verdict at the story's baseline, and `coverage-gates.yml` no longer runs

### Story 117.3: The preflight admits lanes longest-first by the cores they will use

As the operator whose every push and every PR waits on these gates,
I want the preflight admits lanes longest-first by the cores they will use,
So that a green local run still means a green CI run, and both answer in minutes, not a quarter of an hour.

**Type:** feature • **Effort:** M • **Deps:** — • **FR/AD:** spec-pyforge-steward CAP-175 / FR-49 (extends CAP-159 and CAP-166; works with CAP-174, spec-pyforge-core:CAP-8, spec-coverage-gate-independence); lands on AD-1 (wrap, never reimplement: pytest-xdist, buildx, the registry render are the tools' own), AD-5 (provision wraps pixi) and AD-8 (exit-code sole ownership)
**Flag:** `pyforge.steward.preflight_critical_path` (default ON in dev, staging and production: a kill switch; OFF keeps declaration order and the static xdist share)
**Surface:** `src/shared/packages/pyforge-steward/src/pyforge/steward/preflight.py` (`_next_lane`, the admission loop), `preflight_xdist.py` (`lane_weight`; per-admission shares replace the static minimum share), `preflight_budget.py` (`lane_medians(journal)`), `src/platform/config/flags.json`, `src/platform/config/flag-overlays.json`, steward `tests/unit/test_preflight_concurrency.py`, `test_preflight_workers.py`
**Spec:** `planning-artifacts/specs/spec-117-3-the-preflight-admits-lanes-longest-first-by-the-cores-they-will-use.md`
**Given** `_next_lane` admits ready lanes in declaration order and `lane_xdist_worker_map` gives every xdist lane the same minimum share, so a full preflight queues 32–38 lanes on 16 slots and a long lane can start at +1099 s while short lanes hold slots
**When** each lane is weighed by the median seconds of its last five green journal runs (30 s when unknown) and by the workers it will use (serial 1; xdist `max(2, cores // heavy_in_flight)`), ready lanes are ordered `(-expected_seconds, declared_index)`, and the loop admits while the admitted weights plus the candidate's stay ≤ `logical_cores` — behind `pyforge.steward.preflight_critical_path` (ON; OFF keeps declaration order and the static share)
**Then** on the recorded 38-lane full run every lane starts within 60 s of the slot it needs becoming free and the run's wall is within 10 % of its longest lane, with `--jobs` still the upper bound on lanes in flight and the `DOCS_SITE_TASKS` turn-taking unchanged
**And** one test runs the flag ON and OFF; the preflight's frozen exit domain is unchanged
**Status:** done
